## Native player-death reconciliation (2026-10-21; static evidence)

`E:/l4d2_work/m3/remaining_semantics/player_death_reconciliation_20261021.txt` records the complete `336010` / `24B020` ordering and helper ownership. The full-assembly companion retains 2,763 Terror and 204 CS lines; the helper companion retains 5,068 lines, reconstructed without the tool's large-output elision. Both death entries take `this` plus one damage-info reference (`RET 4`); decompiler EBP/ESI/EDI parameters are saved registers. Original, reattributed, and CS force-snapshot damage objects remain distinct. Corpse creation precedes lower-base `LIFE_DYING` and the final incap clear. Director immediate death writers and scripted `zombie_death` dispatch have different positions and owners.

The complete Terror entry and substantial helpers are now authored in external staging, **not integrated death behavior or game acceptance**. `terrorplayer_death.cpp` includes the CCS/Terror entries, force-snapshot ordering, corpse requests, flat-ground blood, pin release, inventory/stat/speech/Director ordering, special-class effects, Boomer explosion, holiday-gift actor, extinguishing, active-state cleanup `3290F0`, active leave `329DB0`, and death-camera callbacks. M3Integrator5 retains production sources, schemas, builds and runtime. State callback/provider and header closure, genuine global Glow, paired ragdoll integration and lower-base seams still gate registration. Build29 remains the last published target; no new original/target game run occurred for this work.

The same direct caller/vtable/target proof withdraws the earlier timed-button tongue erratum: `86BD7` dispatches player vtable `+0x7E4`, resolving `325540` (`LIFE_ALIVE && m_isIncapacitated`), not Ghost or tongue-hanging. M3 corrected the insertion packet; no production `CButtonTimed` existed at that inspection. `source_freeze_20261021c_docs_incap_erratum.json` records corrected lab/capture/contracts hashes. The VMF remains 187,268 bytes with SHA256 `fd02fef8f0d8b5a80f8493c372afa1ce214b67dda22a12b5fa38949e70ea4156`; no new compile or runtime proof is claimed.

### Death-state leaf execution and corrected ownership

`E:/l4d2_work/m3/remaining_semantics/cs_player_death_state.cpp` stages the native state-table owner, public leave-plus-enter transition, internal enter, death-state callbacks and CCS PreThink ordering. The death caller explicitly invokes `State_Leave` then `State_Enter`; it must not call the corrected public transition after an explicit leave. Constructor state-info remains null until the first real enter. Native state4 subtracts twenty velocity units per call on ground, zeros airborne velocity, and transitions at `curtime >= deathTime + 6`; state5 enters DEAD/playback0/NODRAW, attempts the actual target replay, then transitions to observer.

With the production owner's explicit bounded-probe lease, the unchanged transition and state4/5 bodies were extracted and compiled x64 through `vcvars64 -vcvars_ver=14.44`, `/O2 /fp:fast`. `state_leaf_20261021_115302/run.log` reports **18 rows, zero failures**; compiler and executable exits were both0. Cases cover initial null state-info, leave-before-publish, same-state/internal-enter distinctions, invalid-state publication, ground/airborne behavior, unordered COMISS/JB speed behavior, adjacent/equal six-second boundaries, and wait/replay/leave order. `manifest.json` pins source/extract/executable identities and scope. The temporary C++/command/EXE/OBJ were removed; logs and manifest remain. This was controlled leaf instrumentation, **not** an original-executable comparison, full PreThink/state-table/actor execution, game build, visual proof, or M3 pass.

New direct evidence corrects several earlier labels: server animation344 is Charger impact, but345 is initial Charger-pummel victim state—not Tank impact. Client344 is a root-movement float; client76–78 have no matching event writers. Native ClearAnimationState omits byte350, and the complete allocation/constructor chain does not initialize it; no initial-zero or deterministic allocator-residue parity is claimed. The exact server impact calculator, event producers and selector priority are in `player_death_animation_integration_20261021.txt`. CCS PreThink idle exemptions are actual HLTV7336, `m_autoKickDisabled`8592 and the existing `nav_edit` cvar (`5AA670`), not Buddha or `bot_stop`; native `mp_autokick` defaults to 0. Cleanup reuses the real stagger-history vector13204/count13216, distress-response timer11956, and carry `m_isFirmlyAttached`16152, and stops Gurgle/Fall/Heartbeat through the tracked-sound records (`30AD60`), not plain `StopSound`. The team-leave Director bracket D0 remains separate from the suicide interval bracket D1 and the immediate death notification. Native spectator numbering is FIXED3/IN_EYE4/CHASE5/ROAMING6 with no PASSTIME POI; the L4D2 shared enum must exclude POI.

### Engine-capability adaptation: movement trace-list cache (2026-10-21)

Original ledge/movement tracing (`F5700`, bounds setup `F3D00`) optionally allocates a per-move engine trace-list cache through original `IEngineTrace` slot `v76` and uses it when present, otherwise taking the plain `TraceRay` branch. The original and target interfaces do not share slot numbering (the target has two `GetPointContents` methods versus the original's three, `TraceRay` at slot 4 rather than 5, and `GetLeafContainingPoint` at slot 17/`v68`), so original slot numbers are not portable. The target engine's actual `CEngineTraceServer` vtable (`sdk2013_engine` DB, vtable `1803A4050`, 23 entries) has unrelated helpers in slots 18–22 (entity debug, existing-object query, hit-entity assignment, constants 1 and 2) and no allocate/free/`CanTraceRay` service. The port therefore keeps the cache genuinely absent and takes the native uncached branch, with no stub allocator or header slot. This is an **adaptation, not parity**: identical trace results, enumeration order and performance are unproven until a paired original/target movement observation is made.

### Physics2 backend: default-mode parity, opt-in unsupported (2026-10-21)

Client elevator object timing (`10164870`) selects a separate "Physics2" environment when the physics object's slot-86 (`0x158`) selector returns non-NULL, otherwise the ordinary physics environment. The original `vphysics.dll` (canonical DB `45aa54cd8907b62b`, evidence `vphysics_provider_original#800F`, engine factory trace `engine_factory_original#26A1`) really registers `Physics2 Interface v0.5` (ctor `1014BA40` → factory `10064D20`), but its enable guard defaults **off**: only `-phys2`/`-physx` turn it on, `-nophys2`/`-nophysx` force it off. In default runs, legacy objects return NULL from the selector (`10057570`, `xor eax,eax; ret`), so only the ordinary-environment branch runs. The target has no Physics2 provider, consumer or interface; the port uses the real ordinary physics-environment getters and so matches default original behaviour exactly. The `-phys2`/`-physx` opt-in is an unsupported engine capability, not part of parity. Original captures and baselines must not be run with those options.

### Entity steady-state trickling: not ported, stock SDK transmit (user decision, 2026-10-21)

Original L4D2 withholds unchanged ("steady-state") entities per client on a rate budget (stamp at entity `108`, 32-bit per-client mask at `112`, consumer `0EEFA0` inside `CServerGameEnts::CheckTransmit` `0F0B90`, player budget `16AE10`/`16AEA0` with Terror override `308C20`, cvars `ent_steadystate_delay` 5 / `ent_steadystate_enable` 1). It clears that state when a client takes a full update through `IServerGameEnts::PrepareForFullUpdate` (`16DA30`). The target engine never notifies the game DLL of full updates: its public `IServerGameEnts` ends at `CheckTransmit`, all three client baseline-reset paths are internal (`FreeBaselines` `063080`), and none of the ten full-update functions call any game interface (`life_steadystate_fullupdate_gap.txt`, target evidence `C883`/`0791`, native lifecycle `2E08`). Porting the trickle without that reset could leave entities unsent after a full update, so by user decision the port keeps stock SDK transmit and the trickle is not ported. Snapshot contents and bandwidth therefore differ from the original; network-traffic parity is not claimed.

### Stats-crawl ranking: defined behaviour for undefined native reads (user decisions, 2026-10-21)

The end-of-campaign stats crawl ranks survivors through client `26E2F0`, which writes at most 8 records but reports the full eligible count, and returns before writing anything when stats aren't ready or the roster is empty. Two native consumers then read undefined memory:

- **Empty roster / not ready:** the cast list (`2C2FE0`) and the final tagline rank read an uninitialized local count and iterate garbage records. By user decision the port treats that local result as empty (count 0): no cast entries, tagline sum 0. The memorial keeps its native persistent output at `+684` unchanged, and per-stat blocks keep their native status guard.
- **More than 8 eligible:** reachable because VScript `NetProps.SetPropInt(player, "m_iTeamNum", 2)` writes the team directly and bypasses the team cap. The original memorial/cast/tagline then read past the 8-record buffer. By user decision the port keeps the reported count but every consumer reads only `min(count, 8)` written records.
- **Stat-block template rows (follows the same rule):** each native row (`Row`, 5228 bytes) holds a title and exactly 4 player lines, but `2C1600` sets the row count to `min(numplayers, rank count)` with no cap at 4. Stock templates declare 4 players, so it never overflows; a custom template asking for more would write past the row. The port keeps the native count but reads and writes at most the 4 lines the row holds.

All three are adaptations of undefined original behaviour; ordinary non-scripted games with stock content and a populated roster are unaffected.

### `info_director` entity and Director map journal (authored, not registered; 2026-10-22)

`E:/l4d2_work/m6/info_director/info_director.h/.cpp` port the native `CInfoDirector` (factory `594330`, ctor `262910`, datamap `7A51B4`: 18 inputs, 13 outputs) and the Director's in-memory map-input journal at native Director `+1288`. Every input only forwards to its real owner: Survival/Scenario scripted-panic arbitration, scenario stage advance, `PositionFinaleEscapees`, `SetSurvivorPosition`, rules spawn position plus `ClearLastKnownArea`, ghost re-warp, intro state, scavenge ready countdown, the response queue (`ANY` target), `IncrementTeamScore`, `RunScript`/`StopLocalScript` and Tank-frustration enable/disable. Native quirks kept: after a journal record is removed at index 0 (both create and execute), the record that moves into slot 0 is not rechecked. Two undefined native cases are given deterministic behaviour: journal text longer than 255 characters is truncated (native uses an unchecked `strcpy` into a 256-byte buffer), and `WriteToJournal` text without a `:` stores an empty input (native reads a missing split piece). No build or runtime evidence yet; Core owns the Director-side hooks (`terror_director.h#FC73`), M3 owns registration.

### `OnIncapacitated` (`32EB40`): complete body and NULL-attacker decision (authored, not registered; 2026-10-22)

`E:/l4d2_work/m3/remaining_semantics/terrorplayer_incap.cpp` replaces the 34-line production body with the full native order, checked against the 924-line assembly (`on_incapacitated_32EB40_asm.txt`, SHA-256 `c2ad3f78fd2a18bc9eaf41ec4b052ae312e4ec6ec9f26d01d2cf23769db0034d`) because Hex-Rays flagged a bad stack pointer. The native makes one `EndReviving(true)`, on the victim only; it has no `StopUseAction`; it releases the jockey (and recharges its leap by `z_leap_interval_post_incap`) before `EndReviving`. It logs `(INCAP)` through a `CFmtStrN<256>` of the event-log identity and speaks with `PainLevel:Critical`/`SubjectIsA`/`DMG_BURN` criteria under a temporary `FL_ONFIRE`. It records the attacker's incap stat, sends PZ damage message 13 for a player inflictor, adds intensity 4/5, has the other survivors speak, and, when `cm_AutoReviveFromSpecialIncap` is set, releases pounce/carry/tongue/pummel before `OnRevived` and a 5-second recovery countdown (native `11720`). A witch-count guard before that (`test; jg; cmp 1; jnz`) can never call `RequestSurvivorBotAssistance`; it is kept as written. **User decisions:** native `32F100`/`32F283` dereference the damage attacker without a NULL check (reachable when a script's `AllowTakeDamage` table nulls the attacker while the inflictor stays valid); the port treats a NULL attacker as a non-player whose team is not 2. At `32F6B6` the native re-resolves the smoker's tongue victim after firing `tongue_release` and would call `ReleaseTongueVictim` on NULL if a synchronous listener cleared the handle; the port skips that call but still runs `ReleaseTongueOwner` and counts the release. The native blood-pool flag `14984` has no constructor write; its `34A290` reader is not ported yet.

### Client third-person camera: Main decisions (2026-10-22; decision 1 withdrawn)

Both come from `M3MoveParameters`' client `Simulate` slice (`C25FE90`, external, unbuilt). (1) **Withdrawn.** `GetDesiredThirdPersonCameraDistance` (`C26B3F0`) tests DWORD `+9720 == 1` at `C26B5CA` in the Boomer branch. An earlier code-writer census (`camera_9720_zero_proof.json`) found no writer and concluded the value was always zero. That was wrong: it missed a receive-table producer. `+9720` is the networked `DT_TerrorLocalPlayerExclusive.m_nVariantType` (RecvProp `7BCB54`; server `+16428`, unsigned 32-bit), so the branch is live and is ported natively against M5's single client field. A "no writer" proof must cover receive-table producers, including nested local/exclusive tables and RecvProxies, not only code writes. (2) The camera update (`C256780`) checks the jockey-victim (`10056`) and carry-attacker (`10004`) handles, then resolves them again. Nothing runs in between, so on every reachable path the result is the same as resolving once. The native NULL dereference after a failed second resolution (`WorldSpaceCenter`/`GetAbsVelocity` on NULL) is therefore unreachable. The port resolves each handle once and skips that adjustment on NULL.

### Scavenge score/winner arrays past round 5: Main decision (2026-10-22)

With `mp_roundlimit` of 6 or more (per `M6LifeProviderCore`), Scavenge rounds past 5 reach index arithmetic that the native code doesn't bound-check. The rules score getters `2DBE10`/`2DBE60` and writer `2DE670` index the 10-element score array (Rules `+944`), and `2DBCE0` reads and child `286C60` writes the 5-element per-round winner array at `[round-1]`. Indices that still fall inside the declared array, including aliasing into the other team's slots, are defined and kept exactly native. Only an index outside the array is undefined: there the original reads or overwrites neighbouring Rules fields, whose layout the x64 target does not share. **Decision:** at those points only, a read returns 0 and a write is ignored. No new state, round clamp or layout reconstruction; rounds 1–5 and the native overflow wrap are unchanged.

### Spawn placement: zombie-manager dereference kept native (Main decision, 2026-10-22)

`GetPlayerSpawnSpot` (`2E09D0`), for team 3 only, reads `TheZombieManager` (`1085D954`) and tests its byte `+648` with no NULL check (`2E0A1E`/`2E0A24`). `CreateZombieManager` (`35C700`) creates it at server startup before any player can spawn, and an allocation failure there is already fatal in the Source allocator. This is an initialization invariant, not a reachable undefined case, so the port keeps the unguarded access, adding only a debug `Assert`. M3 must confirm that the target creates the manager before the first spawn.

### Hint message queue: SDK implementation is native-equivalent (static evidence, 2026-10-22)

The native queue functions match `src/game/shared/hintmessage.cpp` statement for statement:
- **Queue:** ctor `107A30` (empty vector + player; `m_tmMessageEnd` deliberately uninitialized and never read), `Update` `107CE0` (deadline = curtime + duration, `Send`, delete, remove index 0), `AddMessage` `107D50` (player gate, duplicate scan, `new` + append) and `Reset` `107FC0` (deadline 0, delete each message, count 0 keeping capacity).
- **Message:** `IsEquivalent` `1078F0`, ctor `107B70` (string pointer kept, args cloned), dtor `107C60` and `Send` `107770` (reliable single-user `HintText`, one string).
- **Owner:** the CCS destructor `249ED0` frees only the queue's vector, not the queued messages, as the SDK default destructor does.

Two native bugs are already present in the SDK and stay: `IsEquivalent` always returns false, so duplicates are never weeded out, and messages still queued at destruction leak. No port change.

### Ability debug-victim helper: format decision (Main, 2026-10-22)

Native `360C80` is a free cdecl helper taking the player. It resolves the player's custom ability, formats into 4,096 bytes, and calls ability virtual slot 227 (`+908`): the base and every non-tongue ability have a nullsub (`360630`), `CTongue` overrides it at `36BB80`. It has eight native callers, including both tongue-release-on-death paths (`336010`, `3290F0`). Because it passes the formatted text as the format argument, a `%` from a player name is reinterpreted, which is undefined. **Decision:** the port passes the text through `"%s"`, identical for every text without `%`. The death tongue release also applies the 32F6B6 decision to its own re-resolved victim.

### Client split-screen engine queries: Main decisions (2026-10-22)

From the client simulation work (`M3MoveParameters`). **Engine slot 524** (original `VEngineClient013`, factory `339EB0`, vtable `10359DEC`, slot body `1005E0F0`) returns true only when a non-primary split-screen slot is active; proof `engine_split_slot_identity.json`. The target engine has no split-screen (already an accepted capability gap), so its truthful answer is false: the client's water-particle phase (`C100408C0`) takes the native early return and never writes the draw-only slot. No engine hook or fake query. **Engine slots 516/512** are a per-thread "local player resolvable" flag (getter `1005E0E0`, setter `1005E0C0`). The original engine also sets it itself (default false; false around entity decoding, `PostDataUpdate`/`OnDataChanged` and some command execution; true around network processing and a generic guard; proof `engine_resolvability_writers.json`). Retail client consumers are the Game Instructor locator and the shadow manager's per-view tag. Every Game Instructor root enters its own split-screen guard (true) before locator logic. Shadow readers (`CD220`/`CD400`) accept `tag < 0 || tag == active slot`, so the native −1 tag from false-context creation and a slot-0 tag render identically when slot 0 is the only slot (`shadow_split_slot_semantics.json`). **Decision:** single-slot adaptation. The target treats the local player as always resolvable at slot 0 and omits the game-side resolvability scopes. This is equivalent for one local player, not split-screen parity. It is conditional on the final classification of all 51 reads and 64 sets finding no other slot-0-visible difference.

**Update (census complete):** all 51 reads and 64 sets of the flag are classified (`client_resolvability_census.json#3ABD`). The only retail consumers are the shadow tag and the Game Instructor locator, so the single-slot decision applies unconditionally. **Third-person query** (`CAM_IsThirdPerson`, `1012E050`): the native takes a slot, first returns 0 under a player-controlled-zombies gate (`27C640`), then reads the active slot's camera flag. The port keeps the SDK's no-argument `CInput::CAM_IsThirdPerson` and its single camera flag, adding only the native mode gate under L4D2. This matches the native for slot 0, with the gate's polarity confirmed at the branch before writing.

### Scripted-mode flags: byte 0 is active, byte 1 is enabled (static evidence, 2026-10-22)

In the Director's scripted-mode object (Director `+1608`), byte 0 is **active**: `ScriptMode_OnActivate` (`2799D0`, store at `279A30`) sets it, and shutdown (`279C10`) tests and clears it. Byte 1 is **enabled**: `ScriptMode_Init` (`2798A0`, `2798F3`) writes it from the script's init result. Option wrappers `275B20` (`cm_AutoReviveFromSpecialIncap`), `2759D0` (`cm_AggressiveSpecials`), `275A00` (`cm_SpecialsRetreatToCover`) and `275A30` (`SpecialInfectedAssault > 0`) test only byte 1, then read the option directly. They therefore match `QueryOptionBool` (enabled-gated), not an `IsActive()` gate. `M6ScriptedModeAudit` (`E:/l4d2_work/m6/scripted_mode_audit/audit.txt`) checked 114 sites in 46 files against their natives: 64 correct, 20 wrong flag, 9 extra gate, 21 out of scope (other classes' flags with the same names). That is 16 erroneous expressions: 10 in production, 4 in the M5 selectors, 1 in M6 `NoSurvivorBots` and 1 in an M4 provider excerpt. Escape force, cleared-wanderer chance and spawn-rule selection need **active**; the assault selectors need **enabled** with their strict `> 0`. Per-owner corrections are in the audit; none applied yet.

### Allocation failure is outside the fidelity model (Main decision, 2026-10-22)

Some native code keeps compiler-generated NULL checks after `operator new` and allocator-failure branches. For example, the CCS damage-record adders `249370`/`2494A0` insert a NULL record node (`2493C1`→`249494`) and write list index −1 when node allocation fails (`2438D0`). These branches are reachable only when memory runs out, where both the original and the port fail fatally. The port therefore uses plain SDK allocation semantics with no NULL guards, skips or alternative insertion. Every other part of those routines stays native. This rule applies to every out-of-memory-only branch without a separate decision.

### Fields without constructor writes start at zero (Main decision, 2026-10-22)

Original entity allocation zero-fills. The `CTerrorPlayer` factory (`335980`) allocates 16,448 bytes through `100514D0` → `10001060` (`g_pMemAlloc->Alloc`, then `memset(0)`), and the client's `C_BaseEntity::operator new` also zero-fills. The target allocates server entities through `CBaseEntity::operator new` → `engine->PvAllocEntPrivateData` (the zeroing entity allocator) and client entities with the same `memset`. A field the native constructor never writes therefore starts at 0 / +0.0 / false. That is defined, not undefined, and the port adds no constructor initializer, citing the allocation. Examples: `m_iNumSpawns` (`+9200`), the ghost deadlines (`11492`/`11496`), `m_suppressDeathEffects` (`13193`), `m_wantsToPlay` (`15044`) and `m_incapBloodPoolPlaced` (`14984`). `m_szNewName` is `char[32]`: the layout gap is exactly 32 bytes, and it has no producer, so it stays empty.

### Sequence surrounding bounds when sequences are unavailable (Main decision, 2026-10-22)

Surrounding-bounds mode 7 (`93460` case 7 → `93250`, used by `CTerrorPlayer::Spawn` at `328A84` and by common infected) extracts the current sequence's box through `032DD0`→`02D7B0`. That returns 0 without writing when the model's sequences aren't available, for example a virtual model whose included models aren't loaded. `93250` ignores the result and reads uninitialized locals at `0932B9`. **Decision:** on extraction failure the port takes the native's own explicit no-animation/model fallback (`931F0`, collision-box bounds), with a debug `Assert`. Successful extraction stays exactly native. Mode 7 has one shared owner in `CCollisionProperty` (server and client); common infected's private reconstruction is being migrated to it.

### Formatted text re-used as a format string: standing rule (Main decision, 2026-10-22)

Several native paths format text into a buffer and then pass that buffer as the *format* argument of another printf-style call. Examples: the ability debug-victim helper `360C80` and the animation state dump `33C180` (`sv_anim_dumpstate`), whose text includes model, sequence and activity names. A `%` in a player or asset name is then reinterpreted against missing arguments, which is undefined. **Rule:** at every such site (`Msg`/`DevMsg`/`Warning`/`ConMsg`/`UTIL_LogPrintf`/`ClientPrint`/`vsnprintf` chains), the port passes the buffer through `"%s"`. Output is identical for every text without `%`. No per-site decision is needed; each site cites its native address.

### Client view render: native prerender phase inside the SDK renderer (Main decision, 2026-10-22)

The original client's top-level `Render` (`101BB820`, 405 lines) relies on capabilities the target lacks: an engine `GetScreenAspectRatio(width, height)`, a four-argument main-plus-HUD `RenderView`, and per-split-screen VGUI overrides. The port keeps the SDK's `CViewRender::Render` (with its FOV/aspect/VR/HUD/VGUI owners) and, in the L4D2 build only, inserts the native phase at its native position: set the main view, run the native prerender entity list for local user 0, then the existing `RenderView`. The complete native prerender/add-entity family is ported. The remainder of the native `Render` is an explicit capability adaptation, not parity (evidence `client_prerender_input_native.json#685D`). HL2MP's renderer is untouched.
Also excluded as a renderer capability: the base player's `GetClientModelRenderable` (`10064440`), which returns NULL or the fast-path `IClientModelRenderable` (`this+1592`). The target engine has no such interface and never calls the getter. In the original, NULL selects the engine's ordinary renderable path; it is not a hide instruction. **Coverage confirmed** in source (unbuilt): the local first-person case and the in-eye observer target are decided by the ported `ShouldDraw`/`DrawModel` bodies (`C66210`, `C64000`, CS/Terror `C223440`/`C223360`/`C253140`, dead-rescue helper `C2540F0`) together with the prerender policy (`D5B9`, `C64380`). For same-team players, NULL is only the fast-path fallback: the ordinary draw has no team-equality suppression.
Also omitted as an engine capability: the Terror client simulation tail (`C254810`) writes the owning entity index into each dynamic light (`dlight+64`) for the Spitter-mouth and remote-survivor lights. The target engine's `dlight_t` is 64 bytes with no owner field (`public/dlight.h:43-82`), so the port writes every supported dlight field natively and omits only that store. No trailing field or engine hook. Its visual effect is recorded only if the original engine's use of the field is evidenced; otherwise it is unmeasured.
Shadow weapon with no local player (Main decision): `C_ShadowWeapon::DrawModel` (`221B40`) reads the local player at `221B5B` and dereferences it without a check on the non-third-person path (`221B60`). This is reachable: hierarchy release (`4BE40`) only detaches children, and the CS client removal (`227150`) removes only the stowed weapon, so an active shadow weapon can outlive its local owner. The native renderer (`1BB820`) continues the world render with no local player. The native hides the weapon only when not in third person and the local player's third-person camera is inactive (the first-person hide). **Decision:** with no local player that first-person hide cannot apply, so the weapon takes the ordinary `BaseClass::DrawModel` path: `if ( !CAM_IsThirdPerson() ) { local = GetLocalPlayer(); if ( local && !local->IsThirdPersonCameraActive() ) return 0; } return BaseClass::DrawModel( flags );`. Every case with a local player is unchanged.
Client alpha property (Main decision): the original client's entity data-change root (`4CD30`, reached from the flame's `717D0`) finishes by calling a `CClientAlphaProperty` (vtable `10564B00`) to set render FX/mode, alpha modulation and distance fade (`B8820`/`B8900`/`B8960`). That family is consumed through the newer engine's renderable API, which the target engine lacks; the target draws through the SDK's `ComputeFxBlend`/`GetFxBlend`. The port keeps the SDK alpha backend, migrates the root's other work in native order, and maps each native setter's values onto the SDK's existing render-fx/mode, alpha and fade fields at the same point. Arguments with no SDK counterpart are recorded as omitted; no alpha-property class or no-op setters are added.
Send-property priority (general adaptation): the original engine's send tables carry a per-property priority (for example 128 on the three distance-fade properties). The target's `SendProp` (`dt_send.h`) has no priority field or API, and priority only orders the engine's encoding, not the received values. Every ported send table therefore omits native priority while carrying offsets (from real member layout), bits, flags, proxies and datamap flags exactly. Fade case: the original moves `m_fadeMinDist`/`m_fadeMaxDist`/`m_flFadeScale` into `DT_BaseEntity` on both server and client; neither original `DT_BaseAnimating` has them. The port moves the existing fields there in the L4D2 build, as one paired cutover with all users migrated.
Add-on weapon IDs out of range (Main decision): the client add-on model code (`C226300` → `C30F290`/`C231530`) looks weapon info up with an unbounded index into a 56-entry table (`23157x`), rejecting only 0. Normal producers (`311A40`) send valid IDs, but scripts can write any 8-bit value into `m_AddonModelWeaponIDs` through `NetProps`, so the original's out-of-bounds read is reachable and undefined. **Decision:** the existing canonical `GetTerrorWeaponInfo` returns NULL for an out-of-range ID, and the existing NULL-info path then creates no add-on model for that slot. Valid IDs are unchanged.
Client physics scheduling (Main decision): the original client runs physics on a worker thread between a begin and a gather step (`CPhysicsSystem`, `C161AA0`/`C164DF0`, `vphysics_client_threaded 1`, CPU threshold 4), and defers entity deletion while that runs. The target SDK simulates client physics synchronously (`physics.cpp:434-474`). It does have a job thread pool (`vstdlib/jobthread.h`), but its `IPhysicsEnvironment` exposes no asynchronous simulate API, and the target vphysics' thread safety for off-main-thread client simulation is not established. The port keeps the synchronous backend as a capability adaptation; the simulation steps are the same, only not overlapped. The native deferred-delete queue (`AddToDeleteList` `C100BB020`, nesting depth `BA3B0`, flush `B98F0`) is ported with its real non-physics scopes, such as the weapon bullet bracket `2F53C0`. The physics begin/gather scopes are unreachable here. Any frame-ordering difference from overlapped physics is unmeasured.
Model translucency query (ported, not excluded): the original's render-group root (`42520`, via `6AC50`) calls the engine's `VModelInfoClient004` slot 16 with (model, skin, body). It returns 0 opaque / 1 translucent / 2 two-pass: NULL → 0; brush models from their flags; sprites → 1; other non-studio → 0; studio models force-opaque (flag 4) → 0; otherwise the first non-NULL translucent material among up to 128 for that body and skin gives 2 if the header is two-pass (flag 8), else 1. If none is translucent the result is 0 (`engine_model_translucency_native.json#2877`). The target SDK exposes every input it needs (`GetMaterialListFromBodyAndSkin`, the model cache, the studio header, material `IsTranslucent`), so the port computes it in a game-owned helper (`native_model_translucency.cpp#4DA9`) and maps the result onto the SDK render groups, with no engine change or invented interface.
Discarded uninitialized reads (general rule): when the original reads an uninitialized value but the result is always discarded by a later test (for example the client ID-target smoke check `225A70`, which compares a sphere-intersection output at `22603D`/`22604B` before testing the intersection result at `226050`), the port tests the deciding condition first. The outcome is identical on every path, and the undefined read is removed without initializing, defaulting or adding a fallback.
Uninitialized animation-layer order (Main decision): the client's add-layer function (`3E9C0`) allocates each new layer from the vector's grown memory without writing its order field (`+8`). On first network view-model creation (`6BFD0`), `SetOrder(15)` (`6C01C` → `3D120`) compares that old value with 15 and marks the layer dirty (flag 16) if they differ. The value is uninitialized heap memory, which practically never equals 15, so the original's effective outcome is "dirty". **Decision:** a new layer's order starts at 0, which reproduces that outcome deterministically (reusing the SDK layer initializer if it already does this); everything else stays native.
Client render-bounds cache (Main decision): the original client leaf system caches each renderable's world bounds (`BD760`/`BDF00`), reusing them while a cache-valid flag (`0x20`) is set unless a disable-cache flag (`0x40`, set by `BD550` via root `42340`) prevents caching. The setter does not clear an already-valid cache, so stale bounds can be reused: a real original bug. The target leaf system has no such cache and always recomputes bounds. **Decision:** port the complete game-owned cache in the L4D2 client build, with its flags, storage, compute-or-reuse logic, every native invalidation path and its producers, preserving the bug. No engine change is involved; HL2MP is unchanged.
x87 extended precision (standing numeric rule): the original 32-bit code evaluates float expressions in x87 80-bit registers (64-bit mantissa), sometimes comparing the unrounded register value and storing a rounded float separately; example: client sequence/layer cycle advance `6B210` (`6B3F4..421`, `6B541..566`), which compares `rate*dt*playback >= 1` on the register value. x64 has no 80-bit type (`long double` is `double`). The port evaluates such chains in `double`, stores floats exactly where the original stores them, and compares on the double where the original compares the register value. Results can differ only when a value lies within an x87 ulp of a threshold or a float rounding midpoint. That boundary is recorded as unmeasured, not as bit parity. Software 80-bit emulation is reserved for any case where a measured original/port comparison shows a divergence.
Failed view-model camera attachment (Main decision): with `vm_camera` enabled, `6B960` calls `GetAttachmentLocal` (`1002DA40`) and ignores its result. On failure the angle outputs stay uninitialized and are used at `B4CF0`. The port takes the existing zero-angle path (the same as `vm_camera` off or no attachment index) when the attachment fails; successful attachments are unchanged.
View-model translucency collation (Main decision): the original client leaf system's view-model collation (`C1ED0`) uses the 0/1/2 translucency class. Opaque models draw once at alpha 255, translucent ones once at their alpha, and two-pass models twice: a translucent pass at their alpha and an opaque pass forced to 255. The SDK collates view models into a single bucket. This is client game code, so the port reproduces the native collation, keeping the 0/1/2 class as leaf metadata and applying each pass's alpha through the existing renderer blend state before `DrawModel`. No engine change; L4D2 build only.
Alpha-property revision (Main decision, supersedes the earlier "keep the SDK alpha backend" entry above): the alpha query the native collation uses (`B89B0`) is a full game-side effects calculator, not an engine-consumed getter. It uses a 16-bit seed (×363), packed FX/mode/raw alpha, start/duration timing (`+24`/`+28`) and an override callback (`+200`). The port implements it as plain game-side per-entity state in the L4D2 client, with the native setters' timing arguments now stored and used, and makes it the single alpha source wherever the native consumes it, replacing the SDK `ComputeFxBlend`/`GetFxBlend` on those paths. No alpha-property interface or engine change. The view-model cull query (`BDF00`, engine slot `+136`) is a frustum box cull (`5D600`→`27B290`), mapped to the target `CullBox`, not to PVS.
Cell-relative origin networking (Main decision): the original networks non-local player origins as cell-relative values (root cell width `+108`, cell X/Y/Z `+112..120`, cell-relative origin `+124..132`, decoded as `cellwidth*cell − 0x4000 + value` in `221F80`/`221FE0`). The target transports absolute coordinates. This is whole-network encoding and traffic parity is not claimed, so the port keeps the target's absolute-coordinate transport, stores the received origin where the native stores its decoded value, and ports the local-player `m_vecLastReceivedPosition` (`+6400`) path exactly. The quantization difference is recorded as an unmeasured adaptation.
Engine reslist query (Main decision, capability gap): the original Director constructor and item population ask the original engine whether a resource list is being generated (engine server interface `+496` → `1AA390()[1]`; `+500` = `state[1] && state[4]`), a development-only mode. `+504` is a PC constant false and is elided exactly. The target engine exposes no such query, so each predicate is one named game-side capability function that returns false on target. They are kept separate, are not inferred from the command line, and keep the native short-circuit order (reslist, then `-bugbait`, then `director_min_start_players`).
Stateless Director query receiver (Main decision): the Director constructor allocates a 1-byte object at `+1576` with no RTTI and no state. Its query methods (`2901B0`, `28FD50`, `28FD90`, `28FF20`, `28F340`) ignore the receiver, so the object's only effect is the allocation. The port elides the member and its allocation; callers call the existing owners of those queries (flow-survivor extremes, closest survivor, common-infected count, the random living Versus selector) directly.
Zero-on-new allocation (Main decision): the original Director's RTTI lists Valve's empty base `CMemZeroOnNew`, whose allocator clears memory before construction (`100ED800`). The target SDK lacks it. The port adds it once under its native name, with an allocator that clears memory unconditionally, and derives from it only the classes whose original RTTI shows it. Classes whose original allocation clears memory without that base use a per-class allocator instead (game rules: `27DA60`/`2E22C0`). The Director's car-alarm list (`+1140`) is ported with its native registration and removal, since survivor attack and minigun code read it.
Game-event listener debug ID (Main decision, standing interface adaptation): the original engine's game-event listener base stores a debug ID (42 after construction, 13 after destruction) and exposes it through a virtual at slot `+8`, which the original engine's event manager checks. The target listener interface has neither, and the target engine never reads it. Every ported class derived from `CGameEventListener` keeps the target base unchanged, with no added field or slot.
Prop interactions (Main decision): the original server's prop interaction list has 22 entries (table `1078A958`): `explode_ice` inserted at 7, plus `notify_children` 15, `melee_immune` 16, `tankonly` 17, `mobonly` 18, `forbidden_target` 19, `weapon_collision` 20 and `tank_projectile` 21. The SDK has 15. Under L4D2 the shared props owner takes the native list and parse table; HL2MP keeps the stock list. The Director's car-alarm registry is actually the generic bot forbidden-target registry, driven by the parsed `forbidden_target` flag in the base prop Spawn/Remove hooks. The other new flags are parsed here and consumed only by their gameplay owners.
Static-prop alpha (Main decision, capability gap): in the original, the engine gives every static prop an alpha property created through the client's alpha-property factory, with the raw alpha and map fade distances from the static-prop lump (`1F4BA0`). The client leaf system then applies distance/screen fades to it (`BEBC0`). The target engine owns static-prop alpha and fading and exposes no property, so static props keep the target engine's own path. The game-side alpha calculator (`B89B0`), view-model collation and fade logic apply only to renderables with a game-owned property (entities). The level screen-fade range query (model info `+136`) maps to the SDK's `GetLevelScreenFadeRange`.
Client loading progress (Main decision, capability gap): the original engine tracks each connecting client's loading progress and the server reads it for the transition placeholder players and the survivor bots holding a human's spot (`DT_TransitioningPlayer.m_loadingProgress`, survivor bot `+17228`). The target engine has no such query. One bridge function, `TerrorEngine_GetLoadingProgressForUserID`, returns 0 on target, so transition screens show no progress. Progress is not inferred from signon state.
Client world renderable batch (Main decision): the original client leaf system processes world renderables as one batch (`BEBC0`): bounds and frustum for all, then all alpha queries, then one distance-fade pass using the local FOV, screen-fade ranges, shadow bias, dropping fully faded entries, then occlusion. The SDK computes effects per leaf before bounds. Consistent with the view-render decision, the port runs this native batch as a prepass over entity rows after the SDK's own visibility collection, and the SDK's sorting and drawing consume its results. The SDK's per-leaf effects computation is removed for those rows. `BF210`, reachable only through an original-engine leaf-system interface slot the target lacks, is omitted.
Wrong-type script option values (Main decision, standing): the original Director's string and vector option readers (`262E00`/`4F78C0`, `25D000`) handle some value types explicitly; for example, the vector reader turns nil into the zero vector and returns false, and parses a string with `sscanf("%f %f %f")`. Other types have their raw bits copied as a pointer without a type check, which is undefined behavior. The port keeps every explicitly handled type exactly as native, using the target variant's existing conversion operators. Only the undefined raw-pointer types are treated as the lookup failing (the caller's default or not-found path), in the shared readers.
NaN-sensitive comparisons (Main decision, standing): the original's float comparisons accept or reject NaN/unordered values in specific ways (for example the rescue update `272190` and selector `26E9D0`). The target builds with `/fp:fast`, which may rewrite such comparisons. Where the outcome for unordered values matters, the port writes the comparison in the native polarity and scopes `#pragma float_control(precise, on, push)`/`pop` around just those functions. The global build setting is unchanged.
Handle re-read returning NULL (Main decision, standing): the original often re-resolves an entity handle after an event, speech or spawn call, then dereferences it unchecked. If a listener cleared the target in between, that is undefined behavior. The port re-reads exactly where the original does and, on NULL, skips only the calls that need the target (an event's victim user ID is written as 0); every other step keeps the native order. Examples: tongue release on death or incap, the yell think's noise level (`2CD6A0`), the rescue sequence (`2CD830`).
Loading plaque (Main decision, capability gap): the original client's `AllPlayersConnectedGameStarting` message handler (`26EE30`) unfreezes teams in the player resource, then asks the original engine to hide the loading plaque (engine client `+116` = `1005D360` `HideLoadingPlaque`). The target engine client interface has no such call and hides its loading screen itself at signon. The handler is ported fully; only that final call is omitted.
Loading every mounted copy of a file (Main decision, API-shape adaptation): the original response-rules bootstrap (`2C3210`→`22E240`) asks the original filesystem (`+132`) for every copy of `scripts/vscripts/response_testbed_addon.nut` found in the GAME search paths, not just the highest-precedence one, and runs each. The target filesystem interface has no such call. The port enumerates the GAME search paths with the existing `GetSearchPath` in their native order, checks each for the file, and loads every match, keeping the native bootstrap order.
Script VM table and array helpers (Main decision, API-shape adaptation): the original script VM interface has five table/array operations the target's `IScriptVM` lacks (`IsTable`, `GetArrayCount`, `GetScalarValue`, `CreateArray`, `ArrayAddToTail`). The VM belongs to the engine side, so no interface slots are added. One shared game-side helper performs these operations by running small compiled Squirrel closures (`typeof`, `.len()`, indexing, `append`) and releases them before the VM is destroyed.
Screen-space-only particle systems (Main decision, revised): the original particle definition carries a "screen space effect" flag (byte `703`), and shipped PCFs use it (`screen_fx.pcf` burning/adrenaline, `boomer_fx.pcf` vomit screen effect, `ui_fx.pcf` survivor glows, among others). `CParticleProperty::UpdateControlPoint` (`1512A0`) tests it: for flagged systems, control point 0 uses identity orientation at the world origin. The target's prebuilt particle library does not keep the attribute, so the client reads it game-side from the same mounted PCFs with the SDK `dmxloader` into a definition-name table, and the native test uses that table. Separately, the target particle library does not render these systems in screen space. This is a recorded visual capability gap that the port does not emulate.
Client music elapsed time and mixer layers (Main decision): the original client music asks the original engine how long a sound has been playing (by sound GUID) and sets dynamic mixer layers. The target engine sound interface can report a GUID, whether it is still playing, stop it and set its volume, but has no elapsed-time or mixer-layer calls. Elapsed time is reconstructed game-side by recording the client clock when each music sound is emitted and measuring from there while it is still playing. This is an approximation that ignores pitch and pauses. Mixer-layer calls are a recorded capability gap and are omitted.
Embedded objects allocated separately (Main decision, standing): when an object that the original embeds inside a zero-filled allocation (for example the speech object at player `+12580`) is allocated separately in the port, the separately allocated class derives from the shared `CMemZeroOnNew` allocator. This is a port adaptation, not inherited ancestry from the original. Fields the original constructor leaves unwritten therefore still start at zero, with no constructor or Spawn stores added.
Engine timescale on the client (Main decision): the Witch's client-side smoothing asks the original engine for the timescale (`IVEngineClient+352` = host_timescale × a server timescale factor, original `CBaseServer` field `+272`, default 1). The target exposes that product only through `IEngineTool::GetTimescale`, which exists only with `-tools`. The client binds that interface when it is available. Otherwise it uses the real `host_timescale` value alone, and the missing server factor is a recorded capability gap. No load-time failure and no constant.
Engine indoor/outdoor audio state (Main decision, capability gap): the original client music chooses its in/out playback (`C24B370`) from the original engine's automatic-DSP indoor/outdoor state (engine client `+580`, a byte produced by the engine's room scan `150B0`). The target engine exposes no such read, and the room scan is engine-internal. One named capability function returns the original engine's initial value of that state (before any scan); the rest of the music path is native.
Console variables without readers (Main decision, standing): every console variable and command the original registers is registered in the port with the same name, default, flags and help text, even when no code reads it (for example `music_intensity_override` and `music_intensity_threshold`), because the console itself is observable. The write-only elision rule applies only to fields, not to console variables.
Particle-library internals (Main decision, capability gap): the original particle `Restart` takes two flags (property vs world restart), and the world restart also changes internal collection state. The target library only offers `Restart()` with no arguments, so the game code calls that at the original sites. The flag-specific internals are not reachable. Likewise, the original library's own initializer picks a performance-scaled birth fallback and maximum count (`371C90`), which lives inside the target's prebuilt library with no public interface. Both are recorded gaps. The game-side factory's fallback-name and maximum-count gate (`15C7D0`) is ported exactly.
Single-player check (Main decision, adaptation): the original asks the match-session framework whether the game is offline single player (`25BED0`, used for example by the script `Director.IsSinglePlayerGame`). The target has no match framework, so one shared owner answers with `gpGlobals->maxClients == 1`. That is the target's local single-player setup; it is an adaptation, not parity.
Mission and chapter information without the match framework (Main decision, adaptation): original client panels read the current and next mission/chapter through the match-session framework (`2B3E0`/`2B440`). The target has no match framework. The port answers from the existing mission dictionary, parsed from the mounted original mission files, by looking up the current map; the next chapter is the following map in that mission. A map that belongs to no mission returns nothing, which follows the original's "no mission information" path.
Loading-screen panel animation (Main decision, adaptation): the original engine calls the client's `UpdateProgressBar` (`B2AD0`) during loading, which advances the transition panel's loading animation (`2EB2B0`) and, on a timed period, its stats (`2EB1F0`). The target client receives no such callback. While the target engine reports that the loading image is showing, the panel's own VGUI tick drives the same functions on the original periods. Timing is not parity, because the engine may not tick VGUI during synchronous loading.
Flashlight state gaps (Main decision, capability gap): the original engine's flashlight state has an extra far-Z value (`+56`), and the original muzzle-flash texture lookup (`ECCD0`) passes an extra creation flag (`0x80000`). The target engine's flashlight state has no such field and cannot be extended, and that texture flag means something unrelated on target. Both are omitted. The flashlight otherwise uses the real muzzle texture, the target's shadow backend and its existing shadow-bias settings.
Infected spawn-menu tutorial counters (Main decision, capability gap): the original spawn-mode bar automatically opens the spawn-mode tutorial the first five times, then each class tutorial the first five times, counting in the match framework's per-user storage (`2E1120`/`2E1190`/`2E1280`). The target has no such storage, so the port takes the original's own "no local user" path: the automatic tutorial openings and counters are skipped. The seven spawn menus and manual opening are fully native. No invented persistence.
In-game menu state variable (Main decision, adaptation): the original client reads `gameui_ingame_menu_active`, which only the original GameUI module writes, by name. The target's GameUI never sets it. The variable is registered exactly, and the client mode's per-frame update sets it from the target engine's "game UI visible" query, changing it only when that changes and before the HUD and spawn-menu readers run.
Endless loops from map data (Main decision, standing): where the original would loop forever on cyclic map data (for example a cycle of `logic_relay` callers), which is undefined behavior in C++, the port stops at the first entity it revisits and keeps whatever the walk had gathered. Acyclic data behaves exactly as in the original. When an entity fails to be created during restore, only that entity's restore steps are skipped.
`prop_physics2` (Main decision, capability gap): the original `prop_physics2` entity uses the alternative "Physics2" engine physics system unconditionally (`10195B10`/`10195C30`), and the target engine has no such system. Its factory and console command (`10195EA0`) are omitted, so creating that class by name fails like any unknown class. There is no substitute.
`func_timescale` engine effect (Main decision, adaptation): the original `func_timescale` sets the original engine's separate server timescale factor (engine server `+512`; see the engine-timescale entry above). The target engine has no setter for it. The entity's game-side state, interpolation and messages are ported exactly, and one engine-effect function applies the factor through `host_timescale`: it remembers the value when the factor first moves away from 1, sets `host_timescale` to that base times the factor, and restores it when the factor returns to 1. This is an approximation, not parity.
Integer division by zero (Main decision, standing): where an original integer division can divide by zero because of a console setting or data (for example Tank frustration `34C610`: 100 divided by `z_frustration_lifetime`), which crashes the original and is undefined in C++, the port skips only the step that uses the quotient. Every other check and the native order are unchanged.
Minimal skip for undefined steps (Main decision, standing; generalizes the NULL re-read entry): when one original step would be undefined (a NULL or freshly cleared handle, a static cast to the wrong type, or a value a failed call left uninitialized, such as a missing attachment), the port skips only the work that depends on that value. All other steps keep the original order and behavior. Each application is listed in its packet.
Sorting with a broken comparison (Main decision, standing): where an original sort uses a comparison function that doesn't satisfy the C library's rules (for example the HUD weapon selection comparator `2CDD30`, which returns only 0 or 1), the order produced depends on the sorting algorithm. The port reproduces the original's own `qsort` implementation for that call, together with the comparison exactly as in the original, so the order matches. The comparison is not repaired.
Key-binding change notifications (Main decision, superseded): the original HUD weapon selection's key-binding listener (`2D0C00`) only refreshes a cached display string (`2CE2C0`) that nothing reads. Under the write-only rule, that cache, the listener and its formatting are dropped together, along with 56 interpolation floats that are likewise never read. No polling adaptation is needed.
Hardware-level entity culling (Main decision, capability gap): original entities carry minimum/maximum CPU and GPU level settings (`+1512..1515`, from map keyvalues, networked and copied to child beams, e.g. `C6CAD0`), which the client uses to hide entities above the machine's quality level. The settings are ported as real data. The target engine has no CPU/GPU quality-level query, so the culling test itself is omitted.
Depth-of-field overrides (Main decision, capability gap): the original client depth-of-field renderer (`C1C5E90`/`C1C8CC0`) can also be driven by override values that the original engine and its movie-making tools put in the view setup. The target's view setup is shared with its engine and can't be extended, and nothing on target supplies those overrides. The renderer therefore always takes the original's "no override" path. The `env_dof_controller` entity, global depth-of-field state, all four shader quality levels and the skybox alpha clear are ported fully.
Precipitation blockers inside particle effects (Main decision, capability gap): in the original, particle effects can query the game's ray-tracing environment of precipitation-blocker triangles (environment 1, built at `C1F1E00`) through a particle-system query function (`C15F510`). The target's prebuilt particle library has no such query, so particle-effect operators can't see the blockers. The game side is ported fully: the blocker registry, both ray-tracing environments, the rain/snow point checks and the weather emitter (`C1F2430`).
Uninitialized heap values (Main decision, standing; summarizes the layer-order, activity-cache and instructor-lesson rulings): where the original reads part of a heap object that its constructor never writes (for example a new animation layer's order, a resized layer's activity, or an instructor lesson's active bit), the port starts that part at zero when the object or element is allocated. No tracking state is added, and every write the original does make is unchanged.
Foundry tool (Main decision, capability gap): the original's in-game entity editor ("Foundry") is a separate module the target doesn't have. Its console commands are registered exactly as in the original, and the game behaves as if the tool were not loaded, following the original's own not-loaded path. Where the original would use the tool unconditionally, only that call is skipped. Developer commands that crash on purpose (e.g. `sv_crash`) are ported as-is.
Game particle operators (Main decision, capability gap): the original client adds its own particle operators to the particle library. They are registered through the target library's interface with their own original settings. The original library's shared operator settings (time offset/scale, random strength and about ten others) don't exist in the target's prebuilt library and are not emulated. The ray-environment culling operator (`RtEnvCull`) needs a query the target library lacks, so it isn't registered; effects that use it fall back on the target library's handling of unknown operators.
Client system-level and material settings (Main decision, capability gap): the original client's `cpu_level`/`mem_level`/`gpu_mem_level` settings call an original engine `SetSystemLevel` (`+672`), and `z_wind_dir`/`r_skyboxfogfactor` call material render-context setters the target lacks. The settings themselves, their game-side callback work and the infected-wound reader of `cpu_level` are ported; only those absent engine/material calls are omitted. `cl_crash` keeps its deliberate crash, like `sv_crash`.
Float-to-integer conversion (Main decision, standing): the original's helper for converting x87 floats to integers (`__ftol2_sse`, `C47F370`) uses the SSE2 path on every real CPU. It converts through a 64-bit double to a 32-bit integer, and out-of-range or NaN values become `INT_MIN`. The port uses the same 32-bit conversion (`_mm_cvttsd_si32` for doubles, `_mm_cvtt_ss2si` for floats). This supersedes the earlier "64-bit truncation, keep the low 32 bits" ruling for the stats crawl.
Commands that call missing engine modules (Main decision, standing): when an original console command or callback calls a function on an engine/module interface the target doesn't have (for example script garbage-collection resurrection `+188`, sound-emitter `DescribeSound` `+184`, server `ValidateSession`), the command is registered exactly and its game-side logic (argument checks, usage text, counters, names) is ported. Only the call to the missing interface is omitted. Nothing is faked in its place.
Console variable flags (Main decision, standing): the original's console-variable creation automatically adds some flags (for example "development only" when none are given, and a server bit; mask `0x84280`). Each ported console variable or command is declared with the original's final flags directly in its own definition, so the console looks exactly as it does in the original. The shared SDK console-variable code is not changed, so HL2MP is unaffected.
Server-side navigation editor panels (Main decision, revised): the original's navigation-mesh editing panels (`CTerrorNavUIBasePanel`, `FogEditToolPanel`, `TerrorSelectionToolPanel`) are VGUI panels created by the server game DLL. The target engine does provide what they need: the server DLL receives the engine VGUI interface with the game-DLL panel root (`VEngineVGui001`, `PANEL_GAMEDLL`). Only the SDK's game-side glue code is missing, so the port reconstructs that glue (startup `218A50`, the per-frame VGUI run at `218AF0`) and the three panels. The earlier capability-gap entry is withdrawn. Server demo dumping remains a gap: its file writer needs an original engine-tools function (`+48`) the target lacks.
Add-on content "eclipse" (Main decision, capability gap): the original server tells its engine to switch add-on content on and off by mission and mode (`IVEngineServer+548`, through `CTerrorAddonsEclipseContent` `2DE780`/`2DE7B0`), and reloads the melee and population data only when the engine reports a change. The target engine has no such switch, so nothing would ever change. The adapter is omitted entirely.
Game text dictionary (Main decision, mount-side adaptation): the original engine loads the game's text dictionary by its game folder name (`resource/left4dead2_%language%.txt`), not the client. The target engine derives the name from the mod folder (`mod_l4d2`), so the original dictionary would never load. The client therefore loads `resource/left4dead2_%language%.txt` itself, from the mounted game paths with their normal precedence (updates and DLC first), at the start of client initialization. The original client's own dictionary loads (chat, closed captions) are unchanged.
World-light shadow direction (Main decision, capability gap): the original client asks its engine for the world light that should cast each object's shadow (`IVModelRender+100`, `C0D03D0`, controlled by `r_shadowfromanyworldlight`). The target engine has no such query, so the client always takes the original's "no world light found" path, and shadows follow the shadow-control entity's direction. Everything around the query (blending, shortening, projection) is ported. Re-qualified 2026-10-24 (`E:/l4d2_work/gaps/worldlight_query/HANDOFF.txt#1AC8`, `RECOVERY.txt#C26E`): the engine query (`100D5FF0`) ranks the ordered static-light shortlist of the shared engine light cache (`LightcacheGet` `101A8B70`, flags 1). That shortlist depends on the cache's sample point and first-caller/LRU/invalidation history, which only the engine sees; target `IEngineTool::GetLightingConditions` merges dynamic and style lights and cannot expose it. A game-side reconstruction from the BSP worldlight lump would not be the same function, so the gap stands. This differs from the level-water query, which is a pure function of map data.

### Team-full check requires a valid team (Main decision, 2026-10-22)

`IsTeamFull`/`GetTeamPlayerCount` (`2625F0`) fetch the global team object (`26262E`) and, when the count mode is non-zero, call it without a NULL check (`262633`–`262640`). Census: 3 direct callers. Two pass constant valid teams (`28E1DC`: team 3, mode 2; `3215B1`: team 3, mode 0). The third, `2626D0`, forwards its argument; its 9 call sites (`2D9490`, `2FAD70`, `312760`, `3467E0`) pass literal valid teams or computed teams already range-checked by native gates (`{2,4}` at `2D94B7`; `{2,3}` at `312803`/`312808`, including the retry flip). Teams 2, 3 and 4 exist as global teams, so no invalid team reaches the dereference. The port therefore treats a valid team as a precondition: unguarded native access plus a debug `Assert`, with no return-0 fallback. **Closed.**

### Write-only native fields are elided (Main decision, 2026-10-22)

A native field that is only ever written is elided along with its writes, with the census recorded. That requires no direct or `this`-adjusted read, no embedded-object or copied-range access, no send prop/datamap/receive entry and no client mirror. It has no observable effect. If any reader exists, the field and all its writers are ported exactly, even when the field is always invalid, as with the animation-gate handle `11636`. First case: Terror player `+12992` (written −1 by the constructor `323EE5` and `Spawn` `327BB2`), pending `M6SpawnSpot`'s alias closure. The same applies to a container with no producer: a list nothing ever appends to is always empty, so its readers, clean-up loops and purge have no effect and are elided rather than given an invented element type. Case: the entity flame's vector at `+1112`/`+1124`; the writer classification is complete (no producer, including activation `B2F50`), so it is **elided**. The flame's danger-sound index `+1104` has a real save/restore producer and is ported, with the native one-argument `CSoundEnt::FreeSound` (`101D7E20`).
Unreachable code follows the same rule. Case: the client `C_PerfCrawlListener` (global `106E6BCC`). Its only initializer (`10510DE0`) registers an `atexit` destructor; nothing stores its vtable or links it into any game-system list, and its bases contain no auto game system (`performance_listener_reachability.json#E8D5`, 1,586,025 instructions). Its `Init` and `game_newmap` callback (`258D20`) never run, so they are omitted; the reachable performance gating and sample coalescing are ported.
Closed (2026-10-22): Terror player `+12992` and `+13012` are both write-only and **elided** (constructor −1 stores `323EE5`/`323F31`; `Spawn` `327BB2`). `M6SpawnSpot`'s closure (`spawn_full/sentinel_alias_closure_result.txt#92FC`, raw SHA-256 `9e19b403…177c11a`) covers 1,780,230 code heads: no direct or adjusted access across all 38 actor vtable adjustments, no aggregate base or copy range, no datamap or receive span, no client mirror. The neighbouring `+12996` (`m_burnAttacker`) stays.

### NPC pointer stays stock; non-native entity factories are an open audit (Main decision, 2026-10-22)

The original server's `CBaseEntity::MyNPCPointer` (`51F00`) is a constant NULL. The original has no `CAI_BaseNPC` class at all, and no original entity class overrides `IsNPC()` to true. The stock SDK body (`IsNPC() ? cast : NULL`) therefore already returns NULL for every original L4D2 class and is kept unchanged; an L4D2-only NULL body was withdrawn because it would break compiled HL2 NPC code. That census exposed a real divergence in build composition. **Audit result** (`E:/l4d2_work/m3/factory_audit/audit.txt`, static dependency analysis, not linker-verified): the original registers 558 factory classnames (527 RTTI classes); the current L4D2 server build compiles 505 inputs with 382 active factories. 49 classnames are target-only, 225 are native-only (not yet ported or registered) and 5 share a name but use a different C++ class. The proposal excludes 16 translation units from the L4D2 build only (removing 24 factories) and guards 25 registrations in 12 retained shared units; HL2MP is unchanged. The generated project is stale (it lists a nonexistent `terror_playeranimstate.cpp` and omits 16 current inputs) and must be regenerated. M3 applies the build changes.

### Agent concurrency enforced (2026-10-22)

About 20 agents were live against the user's limit of 6 (including children), mostly long-running M4/M5/M6 agents from earlier sessions plus their children. All except `M3Integrator5` (and its two helpers), `M6SpawnSpot` and `M3PostThinkTeamTailPhase` wrote handoff checkpoints and stopped; the index is `E:/l4d2_work/HANDOFF_INDEX_20261022.txt`. They are revived one at a time only while the total stays at or below 6. During the stop, production `survivor_bot.cpp/.h` was found changed by an unidentified writer (M3, the sole production owner, denies it); those diffs must be reviewed before any build.

### Native NAV command/world/blocker source composition (2026-10-23; admission open)

Three new body-only sources adopt sealed native NAV callbacks099E, trouble7840 and AutoBuild97EE: all29 native callback exports,3 completion exports, fourteen geometry checks, four checklist floods, two suggestion passes and the actual ASM-only rectangle subtraction tail. Static console rows, declarations, factories/schema, existing-root cutovers and project rows are composed separately by the sole parent. Source adoption is not compilation, ABI or gameplay acceptance; the published build29 remains unchanged and M3 remains OPEN/NOTPASS.

Existing TerrorNavMesh/TerrorNavArea/CNavMesh/CNavArea remain the only owners. Population344 is one unsigned one-based ordinal (0 unset), including duplicate-name directory entries; load/save/inheritance and fog1636 share it. Obsolete pooled-name conversion/setter/save-gather code is retired. Debug-selected1612 is separate from base-selected1144, clears after base activation, updates only under z_debug, and deliberately retains its stale off-value.

One ComputeSurfaceHeightChange double area owner replaces the obsolete free flow helper. One SelectNativeRegion public member mutates the real selected set using the existing native heap, real corner lights and CVTT height tolerance. The flow caller retains original-double climb and stored-float drop comparisons in their native order. The actual item-log caller clears, invokes that member, unconditionally appends its seed and consumes the stored selected-set order; no second collector/traversal. Existing ForAllAreasInRadius/NavAreaCollector is specialized under L4D2, sharing its original first-use RNG draw, static marker and global generation with visibility generation. Raw visibility counts retain duplicate/override rows; real iteration marks all local entries and only suppresses inherited duplicates.

Native writable12/rescue13 correction supersedes constructor-only sticky wording: constructor AND DestroyNavigationMesh clear both typed bools, at final selectedLadder=NULL cleanup before the !incremental loaded-clear; incremental destruction also clears them. Reset uses real destruction, with no extra Reset/Spawn stores or x64 WORD cast.

The merged nativeworld2572/7E38 closure retains the existing zero-argument native flow solver, separate endpoints/maxima and real ZombieManager callbacks. It supplies base-before-AAS-before1700 activation, pre-flow elevator binding, constructor flow+0/+0, late rescue-vehicle blocking before round_start_post_nav, region geometry/factories, real breakable queue admission, checkpoint door enumeration and unrounded distance selection, visibility compression phase split, one rescue-room minheap, and native escape-route unordered spacing. The empty-route demo-cache in-object alias remains; only the approved failed spawn diagnostic local is zeroed.

F33E blocker closure retains one registry/connection vector, affectsFlow rather than StartDisabled, no initial block, native tri-state0/1/2 early-completion bug, script signed-corner cancellation, event-only outer transitions and actual highbit writers. The invalid negative team array write alone is skipped; remaining native event/debug/callback steps continue. Physical debug-breakables draws instead of clearing. Existing native RecomputeFlowDistances/timer owns base-first one-pending3-second schedules with native !(timestamp>0) NaN polarity, including c8 hint semantics; no second graph/timer/provider is created.

The genuine prop NAV661D provider is the existing CPhysicsProp secondary interface and actual lifecycle/context scheduling, not a NAV-side substitute. Its return and periodic unrounded comparison remain double, with the native float spills; byte58 saturation maps admitted internal NaN to255. Other games retain SDK float interfaces and bodies.

Well-defined native bugs retained include backwards fog text, lower16-only wipe, selected-set-add versus cursor-toggle, non-world playerclip clear, repeated source-box shrink, reverse surface SOURCE/SOURCE call, never-filled duplicate-connection test, missing-population suggestion reading base Place, first ties and view-radius ordered/NaN polarity. Minimal undefined-step skips, repeated receivers/handles, allocation policy and RNG order remain as supplied. Direct CVTTSS2SI is32; x87 helper conversions use double-to-int32 when reached, never low32 of a synthetic64-bit conversion.

GapConsoleServer alone supplies original metadata and actual native-effective constructor inputs directly, including the29 commands and five external NAV ConVars. The max-auto/flood variables belong to existing nav_edit.cpp; the trouble3 belong to existing l4d2/terror_nav.cpp. No nav_test.cpp owner exists here; shared tier1 ConVar::Create and HL2MP policy are unchanged. Genuine Usage ellipses remain text.

The previous unavailable-GUI omission is WITHDRAWN. Sealed ui_nav_tools manifest1BAF / INTEGRATION87FC / delta5EC6 are fully composed here: real global NavUICommand remains the existing server/NavUI/NavUI.cpp provider, CC_nav_gui delegates once, all three actual panel classes890A and game-owned VGUI bridge/headerB1BE/2403 are complete parent payloads, and the five existing NavUI TUs/public factory glue/VGUI link rows are selected atomically. Existing static panel, gates, nav_edit1/toggle/tick/focus/lifecycle remain that provider, with no local substitute or duplicate gate. Native false fog parameter getters/setters remain false and unreachable work stays elided; this is not an invented working fog editor. All28 native-first NAV command casesE2E1, fifteen nativeworld cases4A4E, factory/NAV blocker cases and six prop-NAV cases remain UNRUN. Build/project generation, ABI/schema selection, numeric/ISA, prediction, render, network, original/target runtime and gameplay acceptance are all UNRUN. Qualified native review4BED also distinguishes the four actual surrounding-bounds virtual consumers from collision-AABB blocker/prop/info-changelevel sites; publishes the existing heap open marker only after successful admission and preserves it across Pop; restores executed FIFO failure direction/endpoint diagnostics; and keeps original-double surface climb versus native stored-float drop tests. Console review7BA2 command fixes retain maximum>0 before the second current-flow sample/division (unordered zero path does not re-query), and the surface helper lower-X clamp uses !(x-low>=0) while its other three clamp polarities remain native.
The current props/c_props roots now select their existing checkpoint-door server class/client recv includes once, retaining their real classes/bodies/schema. contracts/server_console.json1C30 proves z_debug_breakables effective4004; the existing global checkpoint include owns it and terror_nav_breakables retires its static duplicate to extern. The actual GUI nav_fog_edit/nav_gui_rebuild and native z_nav_debug rows come from the same sole8292 console fragment. All fifteen GUI92A4 cases remain UNRUN.

Level-contains-water engine query (Main decision, game-owned reconstruction): the original client (`C2F5E72`, per-pellet MASK_WATER `0x30` trace plus initial point-contents `0x4030`) is gated by `VEngineClient013` slot 159 (`+0x27C` → engine `1005E320`: `host_state.worldbrush->numleafwaterdata != 0`; world brush global `1042FFB8`, set at `100E5F70`, cleared on unload at `1018D473`/`1018DB1A`). The target `IVEngineClient` has no such slot. The engine derives `numleafwaterdata` from `LUMP_LEAFWATERDATA` (36) `filelen / sizeof(dleafwaterdata_t)`, so the client reads the loaded map's lump-36 header at level init (using the lump layout the target engine accepts) and caches `count != 0` in one client L4D2 helper. It is false when no map is loaded. No guessed default.
Public live session query (Main decision, match-framework boundary): `CDirector::IsPublicLiveSession` (inline in `28D170`, `28D1BF`–`28D229`: MatchFramework `10897A48` → `GetMatchSession` → settings `System/network == "LIVE"` and `System/access == "public"`, case-insensitive) takes native's own no-framework/no-session path on target and returns false. The dedicated-server bypass (`28D1AD`–`28D1BD`) stays native in the caller.
HTML/Chrome backend exclusion (Main decision, capability gap): the original client's Chrome/HTML backend RTTI classes (157 rows across the framework, response_rules, view_render, ui_nav_tools and misc_client census groups, enumerated in `E:/l4d2_work/coverage/recensus_20261024/`) (HTMLComboBoxHost, FileNameComboBox and the HTML protobuf messages) serve the original Steam HTML surface, which the target engine/VGUI does not provide. They are excluded with their census (`E:/l4d2_work/m7/hud/batch_c/capability_boundaries.txt`, framework dispositions); no shims.
Client material-system facade (Main decision, capability gap): the original client's `CClientMaterialSystem`/`IClientMaterialSystem` facade exists to answer the original engine/tool interface (local-player tool-record-handle query, PostToolMessage forwarding). The target engine has no such slot, and ToolFramework recording is inactive outside `-tools`. The facade is excluded with no faked calls; the concrete material proxies stay fully owned (`E:/l4d2_work/gaps/misc_client/ADMISSION.txt`).
Material proxy dictionary (Main decision, adaptation): the target's existing `EXPOSE_INTERFACE` entries remain the only proxy registry. Native client `0x13AB40` constructs a case-insensitive symbol table; its comparator `0x3D9A90` calls `0x3D17E0`, which folds ASCII A..Z only (unlike the SDK string helpers' non-ASCII locale fallback). `0x13A990` returns the registered creator's result or NULL for a missing/null name, and `0x13AAF0` replaces the creator for an existing name. Target engine `0x1C7CF0` instead appends the requested spelling to `_IMaterialProxy003` before the client's case-sensitive interface lookup; existing logs request both `ConVar` and `convar`. The existing `terror_material_proxies.cpp` owner therefore installs a supported `IMaterialSystem::SetMaterialProxyFactory` lookup over those same InterfaceReg entries, with native ASCII case equality and newest-registration-first selection. It invokes the real creator with no alias, second dictionary or replacement proxy body. A genuine non-null miss delegates only to the saved target factory's existing non-client/tool lookup; a registered creator's NULL result is returned directly. Release uses the target `0x1C7CE0` Release contract and shutdown restores the saved factory. Source packet/evidence: `E:/l4d2_work/gaps/particle_content/`; mixed-case, unknown-name and shutdown runtime acceptance remains unrun.
Predicted trigger partition (Main decision, adaptation): the native `RemoveAndInsert(140, 256)` becomes target `RemoveAndInsert(140, 0)`. The SDK has no bit-8 client-trigger list and no target consumer queries it. The native game-owned linked-list touch query survives, and the nonpredicted route is unchanged.

### Complete server console and native Director provider composition (2026-10-24; source adoption, acceptance open)

The sealed server console packet1BA8/F006 supplies all433 identities (313 ConVars and120 commands:88 local,3 current M6 bodies and29 already-adopted NAV providers), plus the actual phys2_shoot_speed250/effective6 dependency used by ordinary phys_shoot.
