Native send-prop flag domain (Main decision, standing mapping): native SPROP bits are a different domain from target `dt_common.h`, so native flags are never copied raw. The authoritative 20-bit table is `E:/l4d2_work/m6/world_brush/sprop_flag_domain.txt#C33F`, proven from the native encoder (BC17), decoder (ECF0) and mode evidence (6877):
- Native `0x1000`/`0x2000`/`0x4000` (COORD_MP normal/low-precision/integral) map to target `0x2000`/`0x4000`/`0x8000`. The COORD_MP check precedes NOSCALE, so native `0x4100` is target `SPROP_COORD_MP|SPROP_NOSCALE`, not CHANGES_OFTEN.
- Native `0x800` COLLAPSIBLE maps to target `0x1000`.
- Native `0x40000` CHANGES_OFTEN (priority-64 sort, 1E26) maps to target `1<<10`.
- Native `0x80000` is the tick-count encoding marker (target `SPROP_ENCODED_AGAINST_TICKCOUNT`), not VARINT. Target `SPROP_VARINT` (aliased to NORMAL) is never used for native fixed-width ints.
- Native CELL_COORD `0x8000`/`0x10000`/`0x20000` have no target API and are covered by the absolute-coordinate transport decision.
- Native bits 7 and 10 have no registrations and no codec branch.
Every SendProp and RecvProp row maps through this table, and earlier rows that used another mapping (actor F247/6283/E462 `0x4100`) are re-checked against it.
Target root prediction/interpolation wire rows (Main decision, backend adaptation): the native root send table has no `predictable_id` or `m_ubInterpolationFrame` rows, but target predicted-entity matching and the teleport-discontinuity interpolation path depend on them. Both stay as named target backend adaptations carrying the target's own values, with no parity claim. The native player `m_ubEFNoInterpParity` is ported separately, not aliased to them. The non-native root `m_flGravity` wire row is guarded out of L4D2 when no L4D2 client consumer reads it.

Addendum (2026-10-24): the native-domain mapping is no longer an inference. The `m_vCPPositions` projection of native `0x1000` to target `SPROP_COORD_MP` (`0x2000`) is proven by the direct native encoder, decoder and vector-constructor evidence in `E:/l4d2_work/m6/world_brush/sprop_flag_domain.txt#C33F` (see "Native send-prop flag domain"). This is static proof only; codec, ABI and runtime acceptance remain unrun.
Threaded client physics queue (Main decision, capability adaptation): when native threaded client physics is enabled (`161AA0`: `vphysics_client_threaded` 1, `vphysics_client_threaded_min_cpus` 4, CPU gate and engine global `10781F8C`), native sets the call-queue pointer `10781750`, and clientside physics props created by chunk breaking (`C176B40`) queue EnableMotion(false)/EnableCollisions(true)/Wake until after the job-thread simulation (`164D10`). Temp-ent props pass NULL and apply immediately. The target client simulates VPhysics synchronously and has no such scheduler, so the port always takes the NULL-queue branch. Ops native defers until after the threaded simulate apply immediately. Both ConVars stay registered with native effective flags; there is no queue, functor or overload.
Shader bytecode is not mounted content (Main decision, mount rule): compiled shader combos (`shaders/**/*.vcs`) are runtime code paired with the shader DLL that loads them. The original x86 combos differ in format (dynamic-count header word 8 vs target 288; BSP21Adapter `E:/l4d2_work/bsp21/client_initial/shader_headers.json`), and exposing them broke the target's sky and LightmappedGeneric shaders. Shader bytecode therefore always comes from the target or from the port's own shader-module build. Original layers, including the original `hl2` directory, are mounted content-only with no `shaders/`. Original content precedence (update, DLC3, DLC2, DLC1, base, original hl2 content) stays above target HL2 content.
Generated caches stay out of staged content (Main decision, mount rule; defect found 2026-10-24): target runs rewrote the sound caches in the shared staged loose layer (`I:/l4d2_stage/loose/left4dead2/sound/sound.cache`, `update/sound/sound.cache`, `dlc1`/`dlc2`/`dlc3` `sound/sound.cache`). The engine replaced the hard-linked files, so the original install is unchanged (its `left4dead2/sound/sound.cache` is still 2,860,327 bytes, dated 2019-06-26), but the staged layer no longer held pure original content. Per plan §4.3.6, content-only staging excludes engine-generated caches (`sound/sound.cache`, `maps/soundcache/*`), so the target writes its own caches in the mod write path. Until the staging fix lands, target runs mount a private copy of the cache-bearing sound directories (`-nosound` is not an option; see below).
Shared constant layouts (Main decision, 2026-10-24; `E:/l4d2_work/audits/constant_layouts_20261024/`): game-owned constants follow native under L4D2, and constants the target engine interprets follow the target.
- `m_fFlags`: the target engine never reads it, and engine fake-client state is its own boolean (`0x180062305`). The L4D2 build therefore uses the native layout (FL_FROZEN `0x20`, FL_CLIENT `0x80`, FL_FAKECLIENT `0x100`, no FL_ANIMDUCKING) with native 10-bit player flag precision. HL2MP stays stock.
- `IN_*` buttons: transported but uninterpreted by the engine, so native semantics apply.
- Native player-only trigger gate FSOLID `0x400` (`S10092500`) is a game-callback extension. Target engine ClipRay interprets only SOLID 3/5/6 and FSOLID 1/2/`0x100`.
- Entity handles: native resolves index `&0xFFF`, serial `>>12`, but target engine `0x180205010`/`0x180205750` reads `GetRefEHandle` with a low-16 index and high-16 static-prop tag. The target layout stays. Native raw handle values are translated only in offline/native-capture readers.
- Unproved symbolic identities (387) are left unchanged without speculative renumbering.
Target `-nosound` lifecycle defect (Main decision, launch rule; `E:/l4d2_work/gaps/quit_vaudio/FINDINGS.txt`): target client launches never use `-nosound`. In the target engine (SHA `adba182f…`), `S_Init` (`2BDA0`) loads `vaudio_minimp3` and acquires VAudio002 before checking `-nosound`, but its null-device branch never sets the initialized byte. Both `CVideoMode_Common::Init` and `Host_Init` therefore initialize sound, leaking a module reference. The inactive null device later triggers an automatic `snd_restart` (`S_Update` → `1A43C0`), which deletes the cached VAudio singleton (destructor `17D0` leaves the factory cache stale). Quit then executes through the freed object (AV at engine+`0x2D94A`). The same double initialization re-reads stock sentences and doubles the VOX group counts, which explains the stock-HL2MP `V_MAPNAME9` null `strstr`. Sound-enabled runs quit cleanly (stock control exit 0; L4D2-mounted native run exit 0). This is an engine defect outside the port. The rule is to launch with sound enabled against private writable sound copies; no destructor suppression or cache clearing.

Game-event descriptor limits (Main decision, capability gap; `E:/l4d2_work/fixes/proactive/net_census_runtime/`): the original L4D2 event resources declare three `xuid` fields of type `uint64`, which the target event manager omits from its 386 advertised descriptors. Event name IDs 120 and 390 collide after the target truncates their names. These are engine-side, so the port adds no aliases, renamed events or rewritten resource files. All 65 user messages and every network class/prop pair match natively in build42. The port-only `GiveAmmo` pickup event was removed, and the native `OnAmmoPickup(userid)` producer remains.

BSP21 reader adaptation adopted into production (Main decision, build 45; `E:/l4d2_work/bsp21/build45/`): the hash-bound runtime reader adaptation is the only way the target engine can load original v21 maps, so it moves from prototype to the L4D2 server game DLL. It is installed once at a successful `DLLInit` and removed at `DLLShutdown`, with no reinstall on map change, changelevel or disconnect. Static review found no remaining implementation defect. The prototype passed dedicated and client hotel→native20→hotel→quit with exit 0. The fault after reinstalling within the same process remains unattributed, and the production boundary never reinstalls in-process. Open runtime gates, checked on the build 45 run: the integrated owner's install/remove, Steam reinitializing on disconnect→new server while the adaptation stays installed, and full visual/lighting fidelity.

Native VertexLitGeneric in the game shader module (Main decision, 2026-10-25): original L4D2 model materials (e.g. rifle and arms) depend on native VertexLitGeneric parameters that the target stdshader lacks. `$BASEMAPLUMINANCEPHONGMASK` drives the phong envmap/specular mask (native phong_ps20b c10.y), and `$DIFFUSEEXP` and `$AMBIENTOCCLUSION` feed the lit path. Without them the SDK shader renders over-bright, white/flat viewmodels (PresViewmodel build48 evidence). The port's game shader module reconstructs the native VertexLitGeneric family under its native name, taking precedence over the stock shader per the target materialsystem's lookup order. VMTs are unchanged and no alias is used. `$ENVMAPFRESNELMINMAXEXP` is registered but read only by the generic path, as in native.

Target DirectX level cap (Main decision, capability difference; `E:/l4d2_work/fixes/build48_runtime/presentation/PresViewmodel/native_profile_capability_evidence.txt`): the original selects its vs30/ps30 model shader family when hardware morphing is allowed and the DX support level is at least 100. The target shaderapidx9 caps the support level at 95 (hardware census `1800289B0`, `1800294AF`), so the same native selection rule always takes the vs20/ps20b branch on target. The port evaluates the native rule unchanged and ships the reconstructed 20b family; the unreachable PS30 branch is elided with that census. The PS30-only ambient-occlusion texture accessor, which has no target equivalent, would take native's NULL→WHITE branch.

Serialized game launches (Main decision, 2026-10-25): an initializing Source engine app broadcasts `WM_COPYDATA` (dwData `0x5E740DE0`). The running target's shaderapidx9 window procedure (`27EE0`) handles it with OtherAppInitializing → ReleaseResources, and build51 target runs crashed in that path when original sessions launched concurrently (VMTConditionals dump analysis, `E:/l4d2_work/fixes/build51_runtime/render_crash/`). From now on, original and target game sessions never overlap. Whether the crash in that release path involves port code is being determined separately, since alt-tab and device loss exercise the same path.
Target engine VHV upload during external resource release (engine limitation, 2026-10-25; VMTConditionals dumps, `E:/l4d2_work/fixes/build51_runtime/render_crash/`): when another Source app initializes (or the device is otherwise released), the target engine runs OtherAppInitializing → ReleaseResources before any game-registered release callback. Meanwhile its filesystem-worker static-prop VHV upload copies a complete per-vertex colour payload into a failed-lock dummy destination. That overruns the shader-device and shader-API objects (corrupted vtables matching specific hotel `sp_hdr_*.vhv` colour bytes) and crashes. No game-side code runs in that window, so the port has no faithful fix; the harness serializes launches. Device loss or alt-tab during level load in normal play can hit the same engine path.

Main decision, explicit application of minimal undefined-step rule: native NetProps resolver RVA0x128F00 classifies every proxied DPT_String as string_t, including raw-char fields using proxy0xA7330 (for example m_szLastPlaceName). It then treats the first raw characters as a pointer (GetPropString RVA0x129D00) or writes a pooled string_t into the char buffer (SetPropString RVA0x129DD0). This raw-char reinterpretation has no safe faithful x64 pointer mapping. Preserve native lookup, string_t classification and cache. For a genuinely raw-char representation, skip only the invalid pointer dereference in the getter and return the same empty-string not-found value the native getter returns at RVA0x129DBE (Locale at RVA0x5C6078). In the setter, retain AllocPooledString, skip only the string_t store into char storage, and retain the native full dirty/state-changed step. No raw-array fallback or general API validation layer. Genuine string_t fields, including the native custom overlay proxy0xBF2E0, remain real string_t reads/writes using the target's actual pointer width.

The shipped rd_speedrun_mode.nuc:108 SnapEyeAngles call is not evidence for adding a native API: both original and pre-packet CTerrorPlayer descriptors inherit CBaseFlex and lack it. ### Dedicated startup VPK hashing — launch rule (Main, 2026-10-26)
When sv_pure is not given explicitly on the command line, the target engine's ModInit enables whitelist file tracking. The dedicated filesystem then streams hashes of every mounted VPK v1 archive (about 5.07 GB for the five L4D2 archives) before server.dll loads, with no persisted cache. Original L4D2 does not hash eagerly, and both engines default sv_pure to 0. Rule: dedicated launches (harness and deployment) pass `+sv_pure 0` to reproduce native startup behavior; this is not a pure-server policy change. Evidence: `E:/l4d2_work/fixes/build60_runtime/dedicated_vpk_hashing/`.

A3/A4 MOTION POSE PROJECTIONS: native103F634C..63C6 performs ordered wide (velocity.y*axis.y+axis.x*velocity.x)+velocity.z*axis.z, spills F32 forward/side speed BEFORE GetRunSpeed, divides wide by returned F32runSpeed, then spills F32argument at SetPoseParameter. Separate native velocity queries are restored for each eligible axis and final speed, rather than caching a reference across pose setters. No model/pose forcing, fake bounds or getter/RNG-count fitting.
A5 PLAYBACK UNORDERED SEMANTICS: native COMISS/JNB eligibility excludes ordered sequenceSpeed>=speed or <=0, but preserves unordered fallthrough. Exact !(>=) and !(<=) preserve it. Native COMISS/JBE clamp retains NaN; ordered >12/<-4 clamps remain. Prior source ternary incorrectly mapped NaN to12 or fallback1. No finite/zero-speed validation or sanitization. Runtime NaN instances/payloads are NOT observed or forced.
A6 FRICTION ASSOCIATION: native103FC75A..7E8 projects per-component F32 side/forward velocity BEFORE multiplying by friction coefficient, preserving operand/product/store/add order. Only usedXY forward products are computed. Same forward0/side3 defaults and branch/getter counts; no scheduler/gravity/movement fitting.
A7 LEAN CHAIN: native103FCB70..CBFD keeps lateral projection/divide wide, spills slideF32; nb_lean_max_angle*actualSpeed spills numeratorF32 BEFORE GetRunSpeed. Quotient stays wide through slide, negate, oldlean subtraction, rate, deltaT and oldlean addition; exactly ONE finalF32Y store. Leanmax30/rate3/acceleration500 and existing randomized stored runSpeed are unchanged.

StartActivity/selector/other body methods remain outside. Loco's already-adopted61 Approach/traversal normalize/clamp/governor prefix and matching pop after OnAnimationActivityComplete remain BYTE-UNCHANGED; a SEPARATE precise/FMA-off scope wraps Update only, ending at EOF. Out-of-line providers cannot inherit caller settings. ### In-place testing only; staging removed (user direction, 2026-10-27) — SUPERSEDES earlier run-root rules
All tests run in place. Original-game runs use the real Steam install `I:/SteamLibrary/steamapps/common/Left 4 Dead 2`, and port runs use the repo mod `E:/repo/source-sdk-l4d2/game/mod_l4d2` with the target SDK install. No staged run roots, hard-link mirrors, junction trees, private copies of DLLs, sound directories or configs, per-run gameinfo generation, or copied/loose content layers. Writable state (cfg, logs, sound caches, screenshots, demos) goes where the game normally writes it. Harness outputs and evidence go only under `E:/l4d2_work`. The slow mechanical drives D: and I: are never used for work data, archives or staging; I: holds only the real Steam library.
This supersedes the earlier rules on hard-link mirrors for original runs, private writable sound copies, private run gameinfo, the VpkRunGuard "files beside originals" failure and "never write the original install". The original install now receives the game's own normal writes during original-first tests. Adapting the harness and launch path to in-place runs is the first task when testing resumes; nothing launches through the old staging controllers.
Cleanup executed by Main on 2026-10-27: deleted `I:/l4d2_stage` (mirror install, loose layers, `m2_runs`; extra hard links only, and the real install was verified intact with link count 1), all of `D:/l4d2_work_archive`, `E:/l4d2_work/port_runs`, the staged parts of `vmt_cond` and `bsp21`, and the private build DLL seed copies under `E:/l4d2_work/m3` (except build63). Small result files (json/txt/md/patch/diff ≤2 MB) were preserved first under `E:/l4d2_work/results/{port_runs,d_archive}`. Original-game reference captures, fix packets, reviews and harness sources under `E:/l4d2_work` were kept.
Scratch drive (user direction, 2026-10-27): F: is an Optane drive and the scratch location for heavy or temporary test writes: harness run outputs, captures, dumps, intermediate media, offline-analysis scratch and similar churn, under `F:/l4d2_scratch`. This avoids wearing the E: SSD. Scratch is deleted as soon as each job finishes, because space is limited. Only small durable results (reports, comparison JSON, sealed packets) move to `E:/l4d2_work`. D: and I: are still never used.

### No evidence files on disk (user direction, 2026-10-27) — SUPERSEDES the packet/seal/receipt conventions
Agents do not keep evidence files: no asm/pseudocode dumps, native evidence JSON, census files, reader-frame greps, SHA manifests/seals, closures, receipts, identity/qualification JSONs, screenshots, dumps, logs, media, snapshot ZIPs or derived comparison outputs. A verified finding needs no stored proof. It is recorded as a short text entry: decisions and capability gaps in this ledger, test outcomes as one line each in `E:/l4d2_work/TEST_RESULTS.md`. A fix is delivered as a single patch/diff file (deleted once adopted) plus its ledger entry. Temporary working files live only in `F:/l4d2_scratch` and are deleted at the end of each job.

### Build64: Infected shader vertex declaration (2026-10-27)
`src/materialsystem/stdshaders/infected_dx9.cpp` mapped `$staticprop` to VERTEX_COLOR. Common-infected materials set `$staticprop 1` for authored palette data, but the animated meshes have no COLOR0, so the target shaderapi refused to render them ("does not support vertex format used by the mesh"). Native declarations are 0x103 for the normal pass and 0x401 for depth, with no COLOR0. The port now declares 0x403 and 0x401, keeping compressed vertices, a single 2D texcoord, bump-dependent userdata, and the skinning/static-light combos. The shaders consume COLOR1 lighting, not COLOR0. The other L4D2 shaders were audited and have no matching defect. An in-place hotel smoke run showed no vertex-format warnings, and the commons rendered textured.

### Build65: white pistols fixed — shader helper ConVar binding (2026-10-27)
The original V_4pistols material uses VertexLitGeneric with a base-alpha Phong mask, `$phongboost 100`, no proxy, no GPU condition and no bumpmap. In the port, `l4d2_vertexlitgeneric_dx9_helper.cpp` and `l4d2_phong_dx9_helper.cpp` constructed namespace-global ConVarRefs before ShaderDLL::Connect supplied g_pCVar, so they bound to EmptyConVar permanently. That silently disabled native Phong selection, and the pistol's generic envmap Fresnel [10 40 .4] saturated to white. Fix: function-local static bindings at the consumers, so mat_phong/mat_fullbright/r_lightwarpidentity read their real values. Native VMTs, textures, defaults and HLSL are unchanged; HL2MP is stock. An in-place hotel smoke run on build65 showed textured single and dual pistols, with the magnum, rifle and arms still textured.
Model console messages classified: the 228 "SOLID_VPHYSICS static prop with no vphysics model" lines are native (engine 101F5E20 emits the same level-2 DevMsg and sets SOLID_NONE; those models have no .phy). The error.mdl and door_options warnings also occur in a clean original run. The Infected vertex-format warnings were the separate port defect fixed in build64 and stay at zero. Still open: the unknown "Black" shader and other console diagnostics.

### cl_consistencycheck — engine capability gap (2026-10-27)
The original engine (1033B260) registers `cl_consistencycheck` (flags 0x10000010), whose callback 10092B40 logs ConsistencyCheck and calls the engine-owned client-state consistency verification 100924B0 (force=true). The SDK x64 engine has no such command, and the game's only caller is the server Director (terror_director.cpp:1133), so the resulting "unknown command" console output is an engine gap. It is not silenced and no verification is faked.

### Black shader / vertex fog: forced low-DX gap (2026-10-27)
The native MATERIAL_VAR_VERTEXFOG (bit 31) feeds ShaderShadow::FogMode(mode, forceVertex). The SDK has only the one-argument FogMode and no equivalent flag. The port's Black shader uses explicit vertex fog plus DisableFog on DX≥92 (the native PS2.0b path) and FogToFogColor on PS2.0. Remaining gap: a forced DX90 run on 2.0b-capable hardware with r_pixelfog 1 can lose the fixed-function fog that native forceVertex would keep. This affects only forced low-DX profiles; the shipping target is DX95, and the gap is accepted.

### Cvar ownership gaps against the target engine (2026-10-27)
- `force_disablex360`: the native game flags depend on an original engine-global root (10344C30) that the SDK engine lacks. The port keeps the native game flags and does not OR in REPLICATED. The resulting console warning is an engine ownership gap.
- `props_break_max_pieces`: the SDK materialsystem (18001F730) allocates an early non-replicated placeholder from dxsupport before the game registers its native 0x2000 cvar, which produces 2 linkage warnings. The game's native flags are correct, and the engine bootstrap is not patched.

### Build69 HUD bootstrap adaptation (2026-10-28)
The pinned SDK x64 host (1801A1700) runs InitMaterialSystem (1800F6490), which calls the client's IClientRenderTargets::Init via 1800F68E0. It then runs EngineVGui::Init (1802255F0), which loads Resource/SourceScheme.res, and only after that ClientDLL_Init (18006E6E0). The port's previous viewport-time preparation of the mounted resource/private VFONT came too late, causing 82 KV parser diagnostics. That preparation moves to the game-owned render-target init seam and uses the already-loaded filesystem interface (NOLOAD factory, borrowed lifetime). Scheme reload stays at the viewport, with the VGUI interfaces bound. The same owner shuts down idempotently on normal teardown and on a partial ClientInit failure. No converted files, extra providers, engine patches or fallback scheme/font.

### KeyValues platform-condition diagnostic level (2026-10-28)
The original vstdlib (1000C950) answers unregistered platform conditions quietly with false. Build69 restores this for the five Windows/Xbox names through the existing resolver's native fallback, used only when the symbol is unregistered. The target vstdlib still reports unknown WIN32HIDEF/LODEF/WIDE/DECK conditions with Warning, where native uses DevWarning, so 194 console warnings per port startup remain. This is an engine vstdlib diagnostic-level gap. Condition results are native, and nothing is suppressed.

### Builds 66–69, in place (2026-10-28)
- 66: native animation-event IDs, types and consumers restored; unsupported viewport precreation removed; native Black shader (VS20/PS20/PS20b) added with SDK fog/compression adaptation (forced-DX90 fog gap ledgered).
- 67: auto-smartlook uses the native ClientCmd_Unrestricted("vocalize smartlook auto").
- 68: native server footstep provider, CCSPlayer::HandleAnimEvent, Terror OnFootstep (AI hearing noise, Tank shake), landing and ZombieBotBody callers, shared surface parser, native masked water query via a zero-length CONTENTS_WATER trace.
- 69: native CCSGameRules::ClientCommand override restored (fixes the unreachable vocalize handler; nextmap throttle INT); wrong-role/dead cvar providers removed (cl_upspeed, mp_fraglimit, gascan_throw_force, r_shadowlod); early SourceScheme + 4 fonts prepared at the render-target init seam (other 49 HUD resources late, one plain IAppSystem owner); native Xbox condition defaults; MOTD InitialSpawn restores the native ShouldShowMotD gate (no MOTD on listen servers).
All four DLL targets compiled, linked and were published in place with no errors.

### Build71 console/HUD scheme bootstrap (2026-10-28)
The target engine parses Resource/SourceScheme.res during EngineVGui::Init, before ClientDLL_Init. The original file's native compound platform expressions corrupt that early parse: font aliases come out as literal `&&`, `{`, `Tahoma`, and ConsoleText is missing, so the in-game console draws nothing. The engine later reloads fonts from the retained KeyValues, so a late re-prepare cannot repair this. Build71 prepares the source resources early through the existing CAutoGameSystem owner; it adds no IAppSystem, which was the probable cause of build69's startup failure. The prepared resources live for the whole process: created once, never released by game code, with the owning modules pinned via GetModuleHandleEx(PIN). Target adaptation, not native parity; client.dll static destructors therefore run at process exit.

### Build72 model-decal companion precache (2026-10-28)
Native Subrect SetupMaterialVars (original materialsystem 1000B5F0→1000A6A0) resolves `$ModelMaterial` and forces its full precache through GetMappingWidth. The SDK Subrect skips that companion path, so the first impact created dirt companions with refcount 0, VARSPRECACHED only and `$basetexture` still a string. They rendered as magenta model decals (the hotel room door). The L4D2 material-preparation owner now admits authored Subrect companions and calls GetMappingWidth, retaining and releasing them through its existing level lifecycle. The extra owned reference is an SDK adaptation, not a native AddRef. VMTs, shaders and HL2MP are unchanged.

### Build72 transient EF_NOINTERP (2026-10-28) — cause of choppy player/bot movement
In native L4D2, EF_NOINTERP is transient. AddEffects(8) and a changed SetEffects enqueue the entity index, and the native post-client-message callback drains the queue (reverse order), clearing only bit 8. The target engine never calls that deprecated callback (SV_SendClientUpdates 18013D330 has no post-send seam), so every survivor stayed flagged permanently. That reset interpolation histories and velocity estimates every snapshot: choppy movement, and bots with centered move poses and no walk animation. The L4D2 game-owned adaptation drains the queue at the next GameFrame entry, before any entity work. That is the first game-owned point after the previous send, so the flag is in exactly one snapshot, as in native. The queue keeps index order and duplicates, uses the captured serial to reject deleted or reused entities, and is cleared at teardown. No engine hook. m_ubInterpolationFrame stays an independent counter.

### Build73 level-transition crash fixes (2026-10-28)
- Client (dump 020933): LevelShutdown → C_TerrorClientRagdoll::Release dereferenced a transferred faceplate that the global client entity list had already retired. This is a native raw-pointer use-after-free (native C3162F0/C3176C0/C2634C0). Under the minimal-undefined-step rule, only the corpse faceplate is stored as CHandle/FIELD_EHANDLE; the live-source raw ownership and the surviving-child transfer/release order are unchanged. Target adaptation, not native handle parity.
- Server (dump 024042): Host_Changelevel → LevelShutdown → SurvivorBot destruction ran the SDK Behavior destructor's OnEnd, which reached the checkpoint PopLegsAction/Resume → UpdateEscapePath after the locomotor had been deleted. All nine original Behavior actor destructors delete the owned action tree without calling OnEnd. That native behavior is restored for L4D2 only; stock HL2MP, bot component destruction order and the explicit live Reset/transition callbacks are unchanged.

### Engine gap: LOCAL game-event listener warnings (2026-10-28)
The original engine's WriteListenEventList (1017F1D0) skips LOCAL descriptors before warning about client listeners. The target engine (1801950F0) has no such skip, although registration stores the LOCAL flag. The mounted ModEvents player_hurt, weapon_fire and infected_hurt are LOCAL, so the target prints false "Client listens to event ... unknown by server" warnings. Engine gap; no workaround.

### Client-only particle system lookup — target adaptation (2026-10-28)
The native engine's FindStringIndex (101D9B30) searches the server dictionary and then the client one. The pinned SDK (1801E6ED0) searches only the server dictionary, although AddString (1801E60C0) returns negative client indices and GetString (1801E6FD0) reads them. Client-only precached particle roots (e.g. impact_metal_cheap, impact_wood_cheap) therefore could not be looked up, so those effects never played. The L4D2 client keeps a game-owned name → negative AddString index dictionary, consulted only when the engine lookup misses and cleared with the table at level shutdown.

- NextBot Action::StorePendingEventResult uses the native L4D2 priority policy: the first equal-priority request is kept unless the stored type is SUSTAIN, a higher priority replaces it, and a discarded incoming CRITICAL warns via DevMsg. The SDK template had TF2's most-recent-wins. Verified: c2m2 "RESULT_CRITICAL collision" 2788 → 0 (the door fix also contributes).

### Build81 decisions (2026-10-28)

Cost: about 128 B per formerly-custom operator definition, +32 B per live collection, and up to 8 B per particle when the read mask is extended. This removes the 11 port-only "Unable to prepare particle material" lines for authored VMTs that are absent from the original VPKs.

### Particle dot-visibility normalize: FP width (2026-10-28)
Native C35F4C0 computes (x²+y²)+z² and fsqrt in x87 extended precision, then stores a float. On x64 the port uses double for the sum and sqrt, then stores a float: the closest available width. x87 extended precision isn't reproducible with SSE. No claim of bit-exact FP parity. string_lights (dot min = max = 0) never takes this branch.

### Menu and transition blur (2026-10-28)
- Pause menu: native GameUI G0D2D0 ramps the blur by ±0.05 per UI frame, clamped to [0, 0.85]. It forwards the value through engine E5E3A0 (IVEngineClient slot 165) to client slot 78, which calls TerrorClientMode::SetBlurScale. The SDK interfaces lack that forwarding, so the client-owned BaseModUI drives the existing SetBlurScale with the native ramp and decay. Approved game-owned adaptation, no engine hook.
- Transition screen: the stats material samples _rt_SmallFB0, and EnginePost legitimately replaces that target's RGB with (0.5, 0.5, 0). The port omitted the native final full-blur recapture (C2706D0) before the stats display, which exposed the olive buffer. The recapture is restored; the existing suppression/freeze ownership, mounted resources, blend and blur kernels are unchanged.
- The SDK's surface Think traversal skips hidden ancestors, so the approved client-owned blur driver uses an interval-zero VGUI tick signal, registered after root construction and removed before teardown. This keeps the native per-UI-frame ramp and the hidden-menu decay without an engine hook.
- Native InGameMainMenu::PaintBackground (G5FC10) is restored: it draws only the authored PnlBackground smear through the existing DrawSmearBackground, instead of the inherited Frame's opaque full-screen fill (the flat dark-gray pause background).

- 86: for motion-controlled activities (climb, death, stagger) the anim state calls SetMoveType(MOVETYPE_CUSTOM), as both the native server (S10341C1B → 100632E0) and the native client (C282425) do; the SDK-ported code set collision group 11 instead. This fixes the Tank looping Jump/Jump_Climb on the c2m5 railing.

### Smoker death cloud (2026-10-28)
The Smoker death's authored screen-space smoke (smoker_smokecloud_camera: CP1 at corpse+24, alpha fading over a camera distance of 64–310, lifetime 18 s) bypassed its native distance gate. Dual-sequence SpriteCards stayed on the SDK factory renderer, which lacks the native distance fields, so the haze covered the whole screen even 1200 units away. The approved local card renderer now also handles ordinary non-spline dual-sequence cards that have a visibility CP. It restores the native two-sequence packets: seq2*256 + seq1 + 8388608 as F32, low bytes read; stride-0 attr13 omits seq2. It also restores the native visibility. The existing FP-width and Unsorted adaptations are kept. The dual-capability extension keeps the existing local renderer's query order (orientation matrix before Bind, render list before the capacity queries) as an explicitly admitted residual; no native callback or FP parity is claimed. The particle rate is computed once per batch with an explicit F32 elapsed time. World smoke (18–24 s), cough radius 175 and duration 14 s were already native.

### L4D2 Water shader (2026-10-28, admitted)
The game DLL had no Water owner, so L4D2 water materials (e.g. c3m1 liquids/water_swamp_m1, with flow map, refract, lightmapfog and sky-only reflection) fell back to SDK Water_DX90. That shader ignores those parameters and drew the water as a sharp opaque mirror. Admitted: a game-owned L4D2 Water family (VS20/PS20b; native two-phase noise/flow normals, depth-alpha fog/tint ramps, lightmap fog, additive fourth-power Fresnel reflection, skybox controls, cheap branch, flashlight; all native combinations), with the stock Water_DX81 fallback below DX92. Flashlight is a target pass-composition adaptation: the normal pass outputs the native base once (alpha 1); the SDK's separate flashlight pass outputs only the native flashlight contribution (additive ONE/ONE, no depth or alpha writes, FogToBlack). Cheap water has no flashlight pass. Target adaptation: _rt_WaterRefraction is allocated by CBaseClientRenderTargets outside tools mode too, using the _rt_WaterReflection policy and the existing Shutdown. Native binds fullframe0; the target's dedicated refraction path replaces it without an engine hook.
- Open (not waived): the SDK world shaders write the refraction alpha as the saturating underwater distance along the view ray, not the native vertical depth (native LightmappedGeneric VCS dynamic 4 verified), so shallow shores at grazing angles can look denser than native. The native Water consumer is kept. After runtime verification, check whether the existing game-owned world-shader producers can adopt the native vertical depth (follow-up). Native flow is already VS20/PS2b; this material needs no DX100-only feature.

### Original platform search path (2026-10-28, target adaptation)
Native GameUI (G870A0) mounts the original platform dir and loads resource/vgui_%language%.txt before the console initializes. Console_Title/Console_Submit exist only there; they are absent from all five original VPKs and from the SDK platform. The port mounts the original platform read-only AFTER the SDK platform. Census: 338 original files, 204 same-name SDK shadows (124 byte-identical, 80 different: 73 localization .txt, 6 servers/*.res with incompatible server browser layouts, platformmenu.vdf). The target GameUI and ServerBrowser consume their own layouts, so the SDK files stay authoritative and the original platform only supplies what the target lacks.

### User directive: 60 FPS cap for test plays (2026-10-28)
All smoke/test/playtest runs, port and original alike, use fps_max 60 to save power. It is applied through the launch line or the restored cfg harness and is a canonical runner default.

### Build88 decisions (2026-10-28)
- Video options: the dialog populated its values before the base scheme load. The SDK BuildGroup then deleted and recreated all resource children, leaving empty selections and the advanced-control focus hidden. The native G73F70 post-child OnThink bind/init and the G74F60 initial aspect focus are restored, with a weak child handle. The mode and current-config interfaces, the aspect filter, the original labels/fonts and the build87 mounts/serializer are unchanged (not established as an 87 regression).
- openserverbrowser: native GameUI G15ADB0 owns the command (flags 0, "Opens server browser"). The SDK GameUI omits that registration but keeps its real Servers module action. A client-owned command forwards via the existing IGameUI011::SendMainMenuCommand (approved adaptation, no engine or module patch). The optional page argument (native posts ShowServerBrowserPage) is an admitted target ABI gap: it is ignored, with no extra output.
- Weapon info lookup: native S24EBE0 initializes 56 cached weapon-info handles once (LookupWeaponInfoSlot); a miss returns NULL and no file is read. The port formatted weapon_%alias and called the encrypted KV loader on every query. A missing script (e.g. weapon_ammo) retried the disk forever inside per-think survivor bot scavenge queries. The native once-cache is restored in terror_fx_shared.cpp.
- Jump landing: the native CCSGameMovement duck family (Duck, HandleDuckingSpeedCrop, FinishUnDuck, FinishDuck, CanUnduck, with m_duckUntilOnGround and the descending SetCrouchForJump(false)) is restored in shared/l4d2/cs_gamemovement. The port inherited the SDK duck, so a jump landed DUCKING (eye height 44) and took about 0.27 s to stand back up to 62; native uncrouches 2 frames before contact.
- Special-spawn "Director failed to spawn a special": a native level-1 DevMsg; the original prints it too, while specials spawn naturally. Not a defect.
- Post-processing: EnginePost needs DX >= 92, not DX100. Colour correction count, enables and parameters match the original, and grading/vignette are visible in the port. A paired lossless original/port capture on c3m2 is pending for grain and grading strength (the user observed a difference).
- Particle bounds validity (target adaptation): the SDK particle backend merges child bounds but omits native C36D560's child-validity OR. Build73's native culling therefore hid roots that only have live children, such as the Spitter acid pool (spitter_areaofdenial). A game-owned L4D2 client post-simulation/bounds pass restores the aggregation, keeping each collection's own validity, its numeric bounds, and the culling of genuinely empty trees.

Same frozen view, all four post effects off, integer HDR, exposure 5.77 (native) vs 5.93 (port). Port world ROI luma is far darker: shack 22.49 → 4.42, ground 34.0 → 6.51, sky gap 87.6 → 34.8. Global, not water-only. SceneBrightness92 is investigating. mat_postprocess_enable 0 bypasses only the combined Terror EnginePost pass; HDR pre/post bloom still run; final RGB *= 1 - c10.x (fade-to-black) is a candidate if c10.x is non-zero.
- Build93 grenade-explosion crash: AV in CTerror_OP_RenderSprites::RenderSprite sampling a sheet with an out-of-range sequence (packed 80/24). The SDK particles.lib block callbacks for "Sequence Random" / "Sequence Two Random" emit fractional SIMD values, whereas native dispatches both block paths to the inclusive integer scalar initialisation (RandomInt per particle; native vtables 1065689C/10657094, scalar 1039E6B0/1039F2C0). A fractional seq2 contaminated seq1's packed byte. Build94: game-owned replacements in terror_particle_operators.cpp restore the native collection-RNG draws, scalar block dispatch, and the shipped shuffle/linear state of the primary operator (65-int context; Fisher–Yates reshuffle). Sampler, spline/material paths and the render-policy packet are unchanged. Likely effect: weapon_pipebomb_child_explosion (by fingerprint).
- Global darkness narrowed: with mat_fullbright 1 the port matches the original (ground 140.7 vs 140.7, sky 86.3 vs 85.5), so texture decode and output encoding are fine. In plain mode the port is about 4× darker on the world and the SKY is depressed too (34.8 vs 79.5) and recovers under fullbright. Points at fog / the HDR lighting-mode application rather than albedo; r_shader_srgb 1 makes it darker still. Fog differential next.
- Build93 deployed: the render-policy packet. Its first smoke run crashed on a grenade-launcher explosion: an older particle defect, not the policy packet.
- Build94 deployed (client94/server90/shader91): the native Sequence Random / Sequence Two Random initializers. The Water19 family (WaterView92) was excluded at the cut and moves to 95 as a whole (registered hunks restored to the post-93 anchors; the 9 new files frozen and unregistered).

### Global darkness root (2026-10-30, build96)
apitrace GPU captures (c3m3, the same frozen view) show the port's pixel c30.x = 1 for all main-world, model and sky draws, vs the native live tonemap scale 5.9998. The water reflection view has 0.25 vs native 1.49995.
- Root: in the target SDK, IMatRenderContext::SetToneMappingScaleLinear (materialsystem+0x35C60) forwards only to ShaderAPI and doesn't update the context cache that GetToneMappingScaleLinear (+0x32E00) reads. The original setter (+0x29D00) updates both. The native client saves the getter value before the reflection's ×0.25 and restores it after PopView (+0x1CDFC0/+0x1CECE0), so the port saved a stale 1, and every view after the reflection rendered at scale 1.
- Fix (build96): Terror_ApplyToneMapping submits through ResetToneMappingScale, which sets ShaderAPI and the cache. That path's extra side effect (the SDK chase goal set to 1) is unused by L4D2's game-owned adaptation. Reviewed CLEAR (ReviewTonemap95).
- This also explains the c3m2 water darkness on 95. Earlier static "exclusions" of the save/restore were wrong because they assumed the SDK setter updates the cache.
- Pending, found by the review: native resets the ShaderAPI tonemap to unity before engine post-processing; the port does so after.
- Build96 details: source commit 2d7293ba; L4D2 client and stock client rebuilt (MSVC 14.44.35207), server/shader kept at 95; deploy check 3 DLL / 3 PDB / 43 VCS; client SHA-256 eef7fe4a…; TEST_RESULTS row 273. GPU traces also showed world c12 = (16,16,16,16), SRGBWRITEENABLE on and identical foliage albedo bytes on both sides; the lightmap atlas dimensions differ, so page averages aren't comparable texel for texel. The trace captures bypassed Run, so the exact prior cfg/log can't be restored. Accepted, because this machine is ours; the port's cl_updaterate "30" is intact.
- Build97 (authored, in review): native client +0x1D758C submits the unity tonemap (HDR type != NONE) before DoEnginePostProcessing (+0x1D765D → +0x1C9310). The L4D2 port moves its unity reset there via ResetToneMappingScale(1); the stock late reset stays under !L4D2.
- Build96 verified (c3m3 frozen land pair, all four post effects off): port/original ground 26.85/24.28, trunk 7.03/6.03, wall 35.78/32.36. With mat_fullbright 1 they match exactly (142.73/142.73). Global darkness cured; the port is now about 10% brighter in plain mode at these ROIs, with the live exposure value unobserved (adaptation timing is a candidate). Not claimed as exact parity.
- Build98 (planned): native TerrorClientMode::DoPostScreenSpaceEffects (glow, then transition blur) runs through clientmode vtable 0x105BD4FC +148 (→ +0x276460) after PerformScreenSpaceEffects (+0x1D770B → +0x1D7720); +144 is a nullsub before the viewmodels. The port calls it before the viewmodels and engine post; 98 moves it.
- Build96 water (c3m2 qualified pair): PASS for the surface. Yellow waterline bands gone; green/olive water with ripples, reflections and leaf structure; frame-to-frame motion MAE 4.94 (port) vs 5.07 (original); near-water luma 13.44/12.69, reflected water 46.57/47.22. CPU exposure about 6 on both. Open candidates: an underwater view with white/black cut polygons (eye heights weren't matched; a re-test is pending); mat_viewportscale 0.75 fills the frame in the port but draws top-left 768×576 in the original (a debug path, low priority).

### Builds 97–98 (2026-10-30)
- 97 (commit 05283ff3, TEST_RESULTS 287): native unity tonemap reset before engine post-processing (HDR type != NONE, via ResetToneMappingScale); the stock late reset stays under !L4D2. Reviewed CLEAR.
- 98 (two commits, one build): (a) TerrorClientMode::DoPostScreenSpaceEffects (glow, then transition blur) moved after PerformScreenSpaceEffects, matching native +148 at +0x1D7720 with the main CViewSetup; the old pre-viewmodel call stays for !L4D2. (b) The native underwater fog blocker restored: native DrawExecute 101D3520 calls 101CED80 when DF_CLIP_Z && !DF_CLIP_BELOW, after the opaque world and before entities. It draws a height-clipped world quad at the authored water fog end with the cached dev/black_cheap (Black) material; the port had the cvar and fog-distance producer but no draw. Causal proof: the original at the same exact eye with r_drawunderwaterfogblocker 1 → 0 → 1 goes clean olive → the port's pale left wedge → clean olive. Both reviewed CLEAR (confidence 0.94). Runtime verification pending.
- Build98 underwater VERIFIED: at the exact paused eye, port r_drawunderwaterfogblocker 1 → 0 → 1 gives clean olive (no wedge, no dark cuts) → pale left wedge → clean olive, the same as the original; exposure 6.000 at every bookend.
- Exposure97: the static exposure algorithm (17 bins with power 2.5, sticky target bin, 10-sample weighted history, frametime rate 2 with 1/64 cap) matches native. Query-contract deltas found and being fixed: native edge bins ±1e20, the normal ROI always, full-RT UV dimensions with a viewport-local destination, uncapped max 100, and the default stencil-state restore. Note: L4D2 lumcompare resolves to the SDK hl2 shader, which multiplies C0_Z (the native shader doesn't), so the port keeps a unit C0_Z adapter.

### Build99 (2026-10-30, commit 561acee6)
- The native histogram query contract is restored: ±1e20 edge tails, the flashlight-independent ROI, full-RT UV normalisation with a viewport-local destination, the optional stencil-state restore, and the uncapped maximum of 100 (was 20). L4D2 keeps a unit C0_Z for the SDK luminance shader, which multiplies by c0.z where the native one doesn't. Stock behaviour is unchanged. Reviewed CLEAR (0.94).
- Exposure curves on 99 (port vs original, the same view, forced 1 then released, 30 s at fps 60): both converge to 5.0 at the same ~0.5/s chase (1 s: 2.92/2.74; 5 s: 4.72/4.70; 10 s: 4.978/4.975). The earlier ~8% gap wasn't reproduced; it was most likely adaptation timing at capture. This run was ceiling-limited (targets above the cap of 5), so histogram parity below the cap isn't proven.

- Build100 (commit 2f2e64fe; L4D2 client+server and stock client+server; shader 95): glow selection uses the native entity virtual +348 (GetUseType) as in UpdateGlowSelection C25A150, not GetUsePriority (+236, still used by FindUseEntity). The spawner/upgrade glow-type orphans became real GetUseType overrides, the CWeaponCSBase policy C231C20 moved to GetUseType, and the cabinet's orphan method was deleted (natively it inherits C0195E0 = 2). Reviewed CLEAR (0.95). Verified: the loot outline is no longer saturated blue; it's pale lavender vs native white (second contract below).
- Glow colour residual: native stdshader common 1003AC10 uses raw modulation when $linearwrite = 1 (opcode 11) and linear-colour-space modulation otherwise (opcode 13). The SDK UnlitGeneric always gamma-converts, so glow_color (UnlitGeneric, $linearwrite 1) is washed. Fix in design (build101).
- Builds 99/100 checks: c2m1/c3m3 dry pairs pass with near-matched world ROI and exposure; explosion sanity passes (3 grenade-launcher shots + 2 pipe bombs, no crash, viewmodel normal).
- Build101 (commit 5e80da67): a general native-name game-owned UnlitGeneric (a faithful SDK helper copy binding the installed SDK programs, keeping SDF and depthblend), with native LINEARWRITE semantics (stdshader 1003AC10: raw modulation and sRGB write off for $linearwrite 1; HDR colour scale on RGB only). The same correction is in the owned VertexLit helper. The census found 17 Windows UnlitGeneric materials with $linearwrite 1 (dev/glow_color, the console/rt background, chapter images). GAME_SHADER_DLL was removed from the L4D2 shader VPC so the standard BaseVSShader HW-morph helpers compile. The game shader DLL defines mat_fullbright like the stock helper does. No stock shader edits and no new VCS (all 43 hashes unchanged). Reviewed CLEAR (0.94). Verified: the c3m2 loot outline is now WHITE like native (off-crosshair median RGB 157,156,170 vs 156,155,169.5). Pending: broad UnlitGeneric/owned-shader regression.
- Build note: an MSBuild Clean of the shader project deletes the generated INC/VCS; restore them from git if it happens (never regenerate silently).
- Duck "server doesn't crouch" (build101) was a pause artefact exposing a real native difference: live, the server crouches normally (FL_DUCKING 1, view offset 44). During pause, native CPlayerMove::RunCommand (S1802F0) uses frametime 0 for a paused player (keeping the cheat/noclip/sv_noclipduringpause exception), while the port used TICK_INTERVAL. The cleared paused buttons then finished an uncrouch. Build102 restores the native paused frametime (server only; reviewed CLEAR 0.97).
- User report (2026-10-30): with one human player, opening the in-game menu (ESC) should pause the game automatically. MenuPause101 is establishing native conditions and the port's behaviour.
- Native UnlitGeneric contract (stdshader 6a3915991277cec4; build103 in review): SetupVars 10037800 leaves the registered distance-alpha/outline parameters unwired, and the generic helper 1003AC10 has neither the SDK SDF path nor an Unlit depthblend path ($DEPTHBLEND is registered only by ParticleSphere/SpriteCard). In native GPU traces the smoke/noise draws 6057720/7395225 sample only s0, and the SDF draw 8744121 uses the ordinary s0 + s2 detail multiply with no c5–c9 outline/glow constants. The pre-fix port sampled a stale 1024×512 framebuffer alpha at s10 with c13.x feather (2.13/3.84), which dimmed depth-blended smoke, and executed SDF outlines. Matching c1, c12.w vertex alpha, sRGB read/write and exposure about 6 exclude modulation/HDR. The patch removes those Unlit-only paths/params and keeps the dormant native SDF declarations plus LINEARWRITE; shared programs and the world-alpha producer are unchanged. The capture poses differed by 17 units: draw-state proof only, no paired-image parity.
- Build102 (commit abb4a4cf, servers): native paused movement clock (L4D2: paused ? 0 : TICK_INTERVAL in player_command.cpp). Verified: paused dry/shallow-water duck states now match native (flags 131/643, ducked 1, timer 1000, view offset 44).
- Build103 (commit 7451e1ee, shader DLL only, 43 VCS byte-identical): the native Unlit feature contract (the SDF/depthblend paths removed). Paired runtime acceptance pending.
- Build103 verified (synthetic exact-pose pair, no shader/combo errors): noise sphere median 252 vs native 250 (was 153 vs 245); dialog SDF core 182 on both, with the sharp white outlines gone and soft grey edges restored. Cohort: client100/server102/shader103.

### Build104 (2026-10-30)

### BSP21 admission policy change (2026-10-31)
The BSP21 adapter admitted only the 62 hash-pinned shipped maps. The native engine loads any BSP21 (addons, workshop, community maps), so this blocked the Tumtara test addon. Decision (supersedes the earlier "no arbitrary BSP21" rule): general structural admission for any mounted version-21 BSP, loose or inside an addon VPK. It uses the same verified-layout conversion with strict bounds/lump validation, fails closed with a console error on malformed input, and pins no per-file hashes. The shipped maps must convert exactly as before. Owner: AddonMission106.
- Build107 (commit 7a939968; server107, client106, shader103): the Tumtara failure was the SDK filesystem's VPK v1 single-file addressing (a 28-byte header assumed instead of 12, so payload reads were shifted +16; all 360 addon entries have zero preload). Workshop addons are typically this format. Target adaptation in the BSP runtime owner: a ReadData detour on a private handle copy (v1 AND archive 0x7fff only), with a per-store directory-header version cache invalidated at the CPackedStore destructor, exact module/instruction guards, installation before server/client mission discovery, rollback, and retirement protection including deallocation. The BSP21 62-map allowlist is replaced by structural admission (converted headers, worldlights, sprp/dprp/dplt/dplh validated; geometry keeps the SDK v20 trust level). Reviewer107 CLEAR (0.98). Verified: 62 shipped layouts unchanged, 30 structural cases, 4 addon BSPs. Runtime: Tumtara loads offline (both missions, all 7 encrypted .nuc includes), and c1m1 and c3m2 load normally.
- Build108 (commit ed7458fb; client108/server108/shader103): the SDK KeyValues overload trap. `kv->GetInt(NULL, 0)` binds NULL to the int keySymbol overload, where native reads the node's own value (S29BC90 → S4FA150). It broke the info_zombie_spawn population weights (Tumtara's 18-common button spawned none; any map using info_zombie_spawn population 'default' was affected). A full audit fixed 10 getters in 7 files: population weights, saved script-table string/int/float restore (S223D30), prop ai_addon (S1A5F40), precache dependency resources (S1953D0), credits font (C2C3860), tip icons (C2EAD50), spinner colours (G728F0 → GC1AD0). Reviewed CLEAR (0.96). Verified: 18 HP50 commons spawn like native; c1m1 loads.
- Host audio: the Windows Audio services were disabled on this Windows Server; now Automatic/running with the default S/PDIF device. DirectSound init works in both games, so audio comparisons are possible.
- Build109 (commits 7ad66243 + 40715d4f; client109/server109/shader103):
  - (a) Corpse registries: native server S318BA0/S318C20 and client C251080/C251120 register raw corpse pointers for the constructor-to-destructor lifetime. The port captured invalid CHandles before entity-list registration, so the defib (S3D2D90) and the client defib glow never found bodies. Restored, with ordered destructor removal.
  - (b) The native C_CSPlayer virtual slot 192 (C2223A0) returns NULL to veto automatic player ragdolls. The port rendered a second survivor body; the cs_ragdoll direct base call (C224210 → C3AD80) is kept for special/ledge corpses.
  - (c) Jockey: the ridden survivor's yellow/green light is the cinematic projected flashlight (C2709DF → C100FF570 tests m_jockeyAttacker; literal RGB 0.3,0.3,0). The port had swapped attacker/victim, and six related Jockey-vs-carry predicates in glow alpha/colour/stencil/child exclusions were also corrected.
  Reviews CLEAR (0.98/0.99). Verified: the Jockey light matches the original; Smoker/Hunter/Charger are unchanged; two death/defib cycles each leave one body and revive at HP 50; a Hunter ragdoll is kept.
- Build110 (commit 2907dc6f; client110/server109/shader103): the native C_BaseAnimating prediction map (106BE4B4; 8 rows: skin, body, playback, encoded controller, prev sequence, new-sequence/reset-events/muzzle parities) does NOT predict m_nSequence or m_flCycle. The port inherited SDK prediction of both, rewinding the local player's cycle (the human Tank's throw cycle pinned at about 0.0065), so the bonemerged held rock stayed below ground. Those two base fields are excluded for L4D2; the weapon/viewmodel/C_CSPlayer entries are intact. Reviewed CLEAR (0.97). Verified: the human Tank holds the rock overhead like native and its cycle progresses 0.011 → 0.978; the bot Tank throw holds the rock on both; AK fire/reload/viewmodel are normal; cl_showerror 2 deltas (m_iShotsFired, Tank stamina/timers) also occur natively.
- Candidate (minor): port-only console "Failed to load sound ui\beep23.wav" during the Tumtara L4D1 firearm session.
- Build111 (commit d6941d97; client110/server111/shader103): native CFire::Update (S100D05C0, 0x100D0A5B–0x100D0A9D) always intersects each target's WorldSpaceAABB with the half-size damage box (firesize 16: ±8 xy, 0..8 z), with no player exemption. The SDK exempted players and trusted the broad partition candidates, so survivors near an env_fire burned (the c4m3 top-platform fire wiped bots repeatedly; the original takes 0 damage at the same pose). For L4D2 players and special infected now use the native bounds; inferno/molotov is unaffected. Reviewed CLEAR (0.98); paired runtime verification pending. (Deployed before review clearance: a process slip, no functional impact.)
- Campaign on 110 (offline, Easy, all-bot plus human-led takeover): c3m4 Plantation finale WON (finale_win); c4m1 and c4m2 completed (c4m2 with a real elevator); c4m3 reached the return elevator. Cleared candidates: the c3m4 outro camera (point_viewcontrol hides viewmodel/HUD in both games), the c4m3 healthy mission_lost (no repro; all-bot cvars reset across changelevel in both games), a c4m1 ACK miss (transient, recovered), controller-provoked Witches (the harness now skips calm Witches).
- Build111 verified (paired c4m3): outside pose 0 DMG_BURN in both games over ~10 s; inside-fire positive control 10 burn ticks / 30 damage in both.
- Build112 (client112/server111/shader103): precipitation particle blockers (supersedes the earlier capability-gap ruling that omitted C_INIT_RtEnvCull). The native initializer is restored and registered in the game particle-operator registry. It queries the game-owned precipitation/blocker triangle trees (C15DB30/C15F510-equivalent) with no extension of the prebuilt particle library ABI, no world-trace approximation, and no map or asset edits. All six operator-specific native settings (commit 3540a364) and the scalar/SIMD paths are complete, including the normal filter, finite velocity/lifetime traces, lifespan clipping, the RSQRT/Newton/MINPS differences and the life-stride vector-advance quirk. The c4m1–c4m5 maps use particle rain storm type 6 with 4/6/6/4/4 blockers; rain_storm/rain_storm_outer each call this initializer three times (precipitation cull-on-miss, blocker underside-normal cull, blocker velocity lifespan clip). Reviewer112 CLEAR (0.98). Runtime acceptance PASS (AutoPlaytest18, c4m4, port vs original): all five blocker interiors and the open-door porch are clear after settling, with rain outdoors in both games. A fast teleport between blocker rooms can bring a few streaks in briefly; the original shows the same, because particles are culled only at spawn. (Deployed before review clearance again; a process slip.)
- All-bot controller: a loose mapspawn_addon.nut sets both all-bot flags at server time 1 on every map, so checkpoint transitions no longer drop the all-bot permission. The healthy-team loss after a transition also happens in the original under the old controller.
- Build113 (shader-only candidate) and Build114 / ae47709e (client114/server111/shader114): foliage flashlight dark-flicker (user report: alpha-tested VertexLitGeneric). Two native corrections:
  1. VertexLitGeneric keeps the authored alpha test (GEQUAL) in the flashlight pass (native 1003AC10). Phong is unchanged because native Phong disables it.
  2. Root cause: the owned shaders read live per-draw time, so the base and flashlight passes computed tree sway 390 us apart and the depth EQUAL test failed on swaying leaves. Native caches the rendered client curtime once per frame (engine 10116320 -> materialsystem 1002F1B0 -> shaderapi cache). Build114 publishes gpGlobals->curtime at FRAME_RENDER_START through FLOAT_RENDERPARM_L4D2_CURRENT_TIME (float slot 0), applying the shader-only paused clamp tick*interval + interval - 0.00001f. VertexLitGeneric/UnlitGeneric tree sway, Water and EnginePost consume it.
  Reviewer112 CLEAR (0.98, after one paused-clamp change). Foliage leaf ROI variance: 259.56 on 113, 30.22 on 114; the original measured 18.86 and 39.52. Paused water is stable.
  Residuals: the value is published on every render start, whereas native publishes only with an active game view (no affected path found); the unowned SDK shadow-depth path still uses its own clock; minor paused leaf-luma variation.
- Earlier spectator/brush flicker hypotheses were rejected: bit-stable brushes in both games.
- Campaign (client114): c4m5 finale won, then c5m1 and c5m2 completed genuinely with zero NAV assists (AutoPlaytest19). Indoor rain in the c4m5 safe room is native: a time-matched original also rains there, because the single func_precipitation brush (*153) contains the room and no blocker covers it. Next: c5m3.
- Build115 / 0a517b47 (client115/server115/shader114): sound precache capacity adaptation (Main-admitted; supersedes the build59/60 canonical-domain loss mask).
  - Defect: c6m3 failed with Host_Error "Table soundprecache full".
  - Measured live tables on c6m3: native 19086/20000 entries (15-bit index), port 16384/16384 (14-bit) at the Host_Error. Survivor VO is 15492 native vs 12785 port; non-VO is 3594 vs 3599. Every port key exists natively; the 2707 native-only keys are survivor VO.
  - Native precaches every response wave eagerly (server 0x101D3210, scene precache 0x101CCAE0); there is no native lazy flag. Enlarging the engine table (option 3) was rejected because the prebuilt client keeps fixed MAX_SOUNDS-sized arrays.
  - Change 1: CResponseSystem::Precache() for the primary scripts/talker/terror_player.txt defers on all maps/modes to the real selected-scene/selected-wave late precache (AI_ResponseSystem.cpp:2057–2067). Unchanged: -makereslists stays eager, other response systems and the explicit terror_player waves stay eager, and response selection/RNG and engine overflow are untouched. The canonical actor/map/mode mask machinery is removed.
  - Change 2: response-selected scenes get a scoped allow-precache around DispatchSpawn only when response != NULL, restored before Activate/StartPlayback (sceneentity.cpp:4750–4762).
  - Change 3: native separator equivalence (engine 0x101DD630 normalizes slashes, then lowercases) in CTerrorEngineSoundName. This removes 6 duplicate SDK entries.
  - Ordering, static evidence: speech is unreliable, and SendSnapshot writes the string-table updates before the queued SVC_Sounds; the listen backdoor DirectUpdate runs before packing.
  - Build115b / 19708244: the SDK CEngineSoundServer::IsSoundPrecached (0x1800185C0) compares SV_SoundIndex (0x180143630) != -1, but SV_SoundIndex maps the invalid 0xFFFF to 0, so missing sounds report as precached. CTerrorEngineSoundName::IsPrecached() now does the real "soundprecache" FindStringIndex lookup on the L4D2 server for all 4 SoundEmitter query sites, VScript IsSoundPrecached and the emission guards. The generic query has the same bug but no game callers. Wire index widths: SDK 14 bits vs native 15.
  - Reviewer115 CLEAR twice (0.96). Runtime PASS (client115b/server115b/shader114): fresh c6m3 loads; the real c6m2->c6m3 checkpoint reaches its destination at 3278/16384; occupancy grows 3280 -> 3311 over 655 s; cold selected and direct speech is audible on first use; the c1m1 intro VO plays. This is a capacity adaptation, not native eager-precache parity.
- Build116 / 248404ce (client115b/server116/shader114): nav blocker team domain.
  - Defect: on c7m1 the bots passed the closed tank-car door. The door has no collision gap. The map's func_nav_blocker teamToBlock=-1 (native "any") blocked neither team because SDK TEAM_ANY is -2. A survivor stopped at the door, then the native unstick (ResolveStuckSituation "forced to next path goal") moved him through, and the rest followed.
  - Fix: native raw values are kept. Spawn turns only 0 into -1 (native 0x1048C790). BlockNav/UnblockNav treat -1 as both teams and otherwise use the signed remainder, so -2 selects slot 0 (0x1048BCC0/0x1048C870). Only the nav-area call translates to the SDK wildcard. NetProps reads back the raw value, and the shared team constants are unchanged. All of it is in l4d2/nav_blocker_parent.inc.
  - Reviewer115 CLEAR (0.97, then 0.98 after the raw-value revision). Runtime PASS: the bots wait east of the door through Time 650; the real button press starts tank_spawn and opens the door; traversal continues to the train. Team values 0/-1/2/3/-2, both inputs and late writes were also verified.
- Campaign (AutoPlaytest20): c5 complete with the finale won; c6m1 and c6m2 complete. c6m3 loads on 115b but is not yet won. c7m1 has no chapter credit yet. User report: rain in the c6m3 start safe room (c6m3 has one type-6 precipitation volume, *57, and no blockers). IndoorRain111 found that the SDK prebuilt C_INIT_LifespanFromVelocity differs from native (C399FF0/C39A7A0: native keeps lives already killed (negative), and a no-hit trace kills the particle). A game-owned native operator is approved, pending a time-matched c6m3 pair.
- Sound precache engine limit: patch rejected under the user's rule (do it only if it needs few patches). SoundLimitAudit (read-only, target engine adba182f) counted 14 function detours, or 33 instruction sites plus one factory detour. They cover:
  - two embedded 16-byte-entry precache arrays: CGameServer +0x12298 and CClientState, with 10 array-address constructions in 9 functions;
  - five hard bounds;
  - the sound and svc_Prefetch codecs;
  - the string-table constructor's power-of-two requirement (0x1801E5B30). Native 0x101DE930 rounds up instead, so raising 16384 to 20000 alone fails.
  The build115 deferral stays as the admitted adaptation.
- Build117 / a8d53bd2 (client117/server116/shader114): native C_INIT_LifespanFromVelocity ("Lifetime from Time to Impact") replaces the SDK builtin by name before the first PCF parse; a missing name only warns. It uses the scalar C399FF0 and SIMD C39A7A0 paths and preserves killed/negative and NaN lanes. A clean fraction==1 miss keeps endpos and invalidates only the normal; threshold-rejected, start-solid, all-solid and zero-length traces give the invalid point (scalar -1 / SIMD 0). Other parts: the native mask including water, the endpoint cache and its cursor/life-stride quirks, and the 7952-byte parent-owned cache view. The effect: the SDK builtin was resurrecting rain particles that RtEnvCull had killed. Reviewer112 CLEAR (0.98). Runtime PASS: the c6m3 start safe room is dry (time-matched; the original is clear); all four c4m4 blocker rooms are dry with outdoor rain positive. Pending: c8 basic rain, the mounted-gun regression, the open-door control. weapon_muzzle_flash_minigun_tracers is unreferenced in the native binaries and scripts (dead content).
- Radial menu (user report): with 117, holding Z/X only darkens the screen and shows no items or highlight, although the hidden selection still speaks on release. Root: a non-native CRadialMenu::PerformLayout override set alpha 0 after Update had set 255. Native vtable 0x105CEB54 slot +0x210 is Frame::PerformLayout. Fix commit 3912d448 removes the override; Reviewer115 CLEAR (0.98). NOT built/deployed yet (build118). Also open: the blue release text the original shows is absent in the port. Native evidence points to caption processing (CHudCloseCaption), not server chat. See local://RadialMenu118_handoff.md.

- Final-scope capability decisions (Main, 2026-11-03), recorded from local://MilestoneMatrix.md. These are named exclusions from the M7/M8 exits, not claims that the paths work:
  1. Split-screen: excluded. The target engine has no multi-slot client or command routing. The explicit single-slot adaptation stays; two windows or two cameras will not be presented as split-screen.
  2. Matchmaking lobby, discovery, invites, reservations and server handoff: excluded. Steam CreateLobby is AccessDenied under app 243750, and there is no MatchFramework or reservation protocol. In scope instead: listen and dedicated hosting, direct connect, and the server browser.
  3. Steam Input controller glyphs: excluded, because the target input API is missing. Keyboard/mouse and basic controller input stay in scope.
  4. Service persistence (Steam stats, achievements, Cloud writes): excluded except where the ledger has already proven the target path.
  Everything else in M3–M8 stays in scope, and the remaining packages are listed in the matrix. M8's REORG is the last exclusive step, after all fix work is closed.

The hidden selection highlights by real mouse, release speech is audible, and the camera freeze and ESC/transition behaviour pass. The blue release caption is still missing.
- Caption root (RadialCaption119, Reviewer115 CLEAR 0.98, not yet built): the port server serialized the SDK CloseCaption message (token string + short duration + flag byte), while the L4D2 client expects CRC32 + 15-bit duration + player bit (native server 0x101CA120: gender-suffix fallback, shared no-repeat gate, RemoveBots). The client OnDataArrived file-index check is restored (native 0x1011A990). Also found: the live ClientScheme carries the SDK caption font aliases, so the native CloseCaption_Normal lookup returns 0; still under investigation together with the radial visual.
- Stability120: the c1m3 zero-position producer is native (CTerrorCheckpoint::GetSpawnPosition zeroes its output on failure, and GetPlayerSpawnSpot teleports to it: original S2588B0/S2E09D0). No fallback patch.

Port-side per-rule parity for ghost, attack, team swap and second half was not individually recorded.

- M4 STATE (State136, client122/server124/shader114): all eight cases MATCH the native original, bounded. No fix needed.
  - Temp-health decay is 0.27/s; the pills/adrenaline caps match.
  - Revive takes 5 s and leaves 1+30. Native max incaps is 2: the second revive gives black-and-white, and the next incap kills.
  - Bleed-out takes ~103.3 s in both.
  - A hit doesn't cancel a revive, moving away does, and competing revivers end with one owner.
  - Medkit: interrupting it heals nothing; it heals 40->88 and takes black-and-white to 80. The native heal_end subject quirk is kept.
  - Defib: 3 s charge, 50 HP; when two users compete for one corpse, only the first consumes their defib.
  - Ledge: 100.933 s, with a 70.49 terminal fall.
  - Falls: 256 units -> 11.11 damage, 384 units -> 177.78 damage (incap).
- Arms (user report: pistol without hands while idle/in-eye on c5m1): Arms137's native init flag correction (renderWithViewModels true, native C28884D) is CLEAR but not causal. The cause is still under investigation.
- Client123 / d65c0a3f (client123/server124/shader114): the viewmodel arms attachment now initializes with renderWithViewModels true (native C28884D). Reviewer115 CLEAR. This is native parity only. The arms keep EF_NODRAW and draw inside the viewmodel pass either way.
  - User report (pistol with no hands while idle on c5m1): NOT REPRODUCED on 122 or 123. Checked: own view, automatic-AFK idle with in-eye Nick/Ellis, spec_next through all four characters, takeover back, idle across a changelevel. Hands were visible every time.
  - The source/native arms draw/update/character gates match. A sole human can't idle through ESC or go_away (native guard); the user's chat lines imply another human or an AFK timeout.
  - RESOLVED as native/fixture behaviour (Arms139). The screenshot came from a two-client harness run, where the REMOTE client was idle and in-eye on a bot. The arms' UpdateClientSideAnimation returns early when nb_stop is set (native C259790 does the same), and the harness sets nb_stop 1 before the remote connects, so the remote's never-owned bot viewmodel never creates arms. The original also loses the remote idle/in-eye arms under nb_stop 1 and restores them under nb_stop 0; hosts keep their hands. No port fix.
- M4 ITEMS (Items138 + Bile140): two native fixes, then all eight cases against the original.

Player-owned molotov fire had been throttled to 1 HP/0.5 s; it is now 1 HP/0.2 s, matching the original. Reviewer115 CLEAR (0.99).
  - Server126 / a9aaaaee: native common-infected IT pursuit is restored: the closest-IT override with pipe-bomb precedence (S4129D7), non-IT-only survivor retargeting (S4130E5), and the IT-parent contact pushaway (S411AF0/S4EA090). Commons now damage a biled Tank: 6000 -> 5320, vs native 6000 -> 5460, vs 6000 -> 6000 before the fix. Reviewer115 CLEAR (0.99).

    - pipe bomb: lure and blast, 6 s fuse, damage classes 1000/5/0, teammate friendly fire 0/1 on Easy/Normal;
    - molotov: 5 HP/s over a ~15 s area;

    - upgrade packs: four uses, 50 rounds, consumed per real shot;

    - pistol friendly fire 0/1/3/17 across the four difficulties (exact);

- Server127 / 67c0e97f (client123/server127/shader114): the server viewmodel layer playback is now 1.0 (native S07FBF0 at 0x1007FE88 writes it unconditionally for the selected layer). The port had inherited the stopped main-sequence rate 0, which froze the chainsaw in STARTUP: no damage and no fuel use in plain coop. The client keeps the effective rate, as native C022F50 does. - Client126/Server130 / d9146703 (client126/server130/shader114): Jockey steering fixed.
  - Root: native Terror S2D33F0/C277A50 sits in vtable slot 188, which is stuck-check permission. The port had attached it to CanAccelerate (slot 104), so a ridden victim never accelerated on the ground.
  - Fix: a separate L4D2-guarded CanCheckStuck virtual, used by PlayerMove and CheckStuck (S0FC610/S0F8780); acceleration inherits the CS policy. Reviewer115 CLEAR (0.99).
  - PASS: the ridden victim moves for both a human and a bot victim (~116–118 u/s sampled vs the original's 119.5), with max speed following 220 x health/maxhealth, and resists opposing steering. Regression: a Hunter victim stays pinned (74/74 samples, zero velocity).
  - Native path: the rider's usercmd is captured into m_jockeyRaw*, and the victim's own usercmd is blended server-side by ApplyJockeyControl.

  - Witch bounded matches: proximity rage ~99–101 u; pistol-noise rage ~280–340 u; nearest-target selection; Normal one claw -> incap; execution 10x30 then retreat; burning death ~15 s; crown with oneshot=1.

- Shader115 / 561c1480 (client126/server130/shader115, 45 VCS): game-owned native-name UnlitTwoTexture.
  - Defect: the human SI ability-cooldown ring rendered as an opaque red/black square.
  - Root: the SDK PS hardcodes alpha=1. Native stdshader S37FA0 selects TRANSLUCENT by MATERIAL_VAR_TRANSLUCENT (bit 21) and outputs tex0*tex1*c1 alpha (GPU trace: native PS mul RGBA vs port mov r0.w=1).
  - Two new VCS (PS20/PS20b, 48 combos via real FXC); the old 43 are byte-unchanged. Reviewer115 CLEAR (0.99, after a VERTEXALPHA -> TRANSLUCENT correction).
  - PASS: a real human Smoker's 3 s cooldown shows the native transparent ring; the c1m1 HUD/world regression is clear.
